non-passing streak, and $H=(r_1,\ldots,r_m)$ is the ordered list of
stage result values. The flag $s\in\{0,1\}$ indicates a pending
suggestion; $z\in\{0,1\}$ indicates whether the single stage extension
has been granted; $x\in\mathbb{N}_0$ counts evaluable attempts consumed
\emph{after} that grant, with $0\le x\le e_p$ and $x=0$ when $z=0$.
Finally, $f\in L$ is the furthest stage reached in the lesson.
On a stage change (either normal pass-based progression or an
authorized adult adjustment), the stage-specific counts, streak, history,
pending suggestion and extension state reset to zero or empty; $f$ retains
the furthest stage reached so far. \emph{No child ability estimate} is
part of this state (BR-006).

\paragraph{Drawing input and attempt classification.}
An attempt is a finite ordered list of stylus strokes
$X=(x_1,\ldots,x_j)$, where every $x_i$ is an ordered list of
reported points. Lifting the stylus closes one $x_i$, not the attempt.
The terminal event $\operatorname{Finalize}(X)$ denotes the end of input
collection for an attempt. This abstract event does not prescribe a
particular user-interface control. A stylus lift alone does not finalize
the attempt; interruption before finalization cannot produce a passed
result (FR-LRN-008--010).

Let $\operatorname{Valid}_{\ell,l}(X)$ mean that all input required to
interpret the strokes is available, and let
$\operatorname{Complete}_{\ell,l}(X)$ mean that every required part of
the drawing is present according to the lesson's fixed stage criteria.
These functions use the fixed completeness criteria of the selected lesson
and stage. Stage result values use the normalized 0--100 scale specified in
Table~\ref{tab:goalconst}; the actual scoring function is defined for
each lesson's drawing criteria rather than as an assessment of a learner.
Define
\[
C_{\ell,l}(X)=
\begin{cases}
\textsf{invalid} & \neg\operatorname{Valid}_{\ell,l}(X),\\
\textsf{complete} & \operatorname{Valid}_{\ell,l}(X)\land
                      \operatorname{Complete}_{\ell,l}(X),\\
\textsf{incomplete} & \text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\]
The guidance classification uses the level of the actual attempt:
\[
G(l)=\begin{cases}
\textsf{assisted} & l\in\{T,E\},\\
\textsf{independent} & l=I.
\end{cases}
\]
Let $v_{\ell,l}(X)\in[0,100]$ be the normalized stage result of an
evaluable drawing. Its threshold is
$\theta_p=\texttt{STAGE\_\allowbreak PASS}(p)$, taken from the adult's approved
profile value (or midpoint default under BR-008). The pass predicate is
\[
\operatorname{Pass}(X,l,p)\iff
C_{\ell,l}(X)=\textsf{complete}
\land v_{\ell,l}(X)\ge\theta_p.
\]
A missing, malformed or interrupted attempt never satisfies this
predicate. Non-passing but evaluable attempts contribute to the
unsuccessful-attempt count. Invalid or interrupted input is never interpreted as success and does
not contribute to a stage's evaluated-attempt or unsuccessful-attempt
counters. The presented feedback is selected from the fixed set of
observations in BR-007 rather than generated advice.

\paragraph{Improvement and suggestion predicates.}
Use the constants defined in Section~\ref{sec:constants}:
\[
\begin{aligned}
d_p &= \texttt{DIFFICULTY\_\allowbreak STREAK}(p), &
 b_p &= \texttt{ATTEMPT\_\allowbreak LIMIT}(p),\\
w_p &= \texttt{IMPROVE\_\allowbreak WINDOW}(p), &
 e_p &= \texttt{ATTEMPT\_\allowbreak EXTENSION}(p),\\
 h_p &= \texttt{ATTEMPT\_\allowbreak LIMIT\_\allowbreak MAX}(p), &
 \delta &= \texttt{IMPROVE\_\allowbreak MARGIN}.
\end{aligned}
\]
For $H=(r_1,\ldots,r_m)$, define the total improvement predicate
\[
\operatorname{Improving}(H,p)=
\begin{cases}
\textsf{false}, & m<2w_p,\\
\displaystyle
\left(\frac{1}{w_p}\sum_{i=m-w_p+1}^{m} r_i
-\frac{1}{w_p}\sum_{i=m-2w_p+1}^{m-w_p}r_i\ge\delta\right),
& m\ge 2w_p.
\end{cases}
\]
Insufficient history therefore does not count as improvement and does
not delay a difficulty suggestion. For the default $w_p=2$, the first
comparison uses four evaluable attempts; a shorter history still allows
the suggestion at the selected failure-streak threshold.

For an eligible return to a preceding guidance level, the suggestion
trigger is
\[
\operatorname{SuggestBack}(\sigma)\iff
q=\textsf{active}\land s=0\land l\in\{E,I\}\land k\ge d_p
\land\neg\operatorname{Improving}(H,p).
\]
The rule applies to drawing levels $E$ and $I$; $T$ has no preceding
drawing level. A suggestion changes $q$ to $\textsf{suggested}$ and $s$
to $1$, but \emph{does not change $l$}. Declining or ignoring a suggestion
resets the streak counter $k$ to zero, preventing an immediate re-offer;
a new suggestion requires another $d_p$ consecutive failures.

\paragraph{State-transition rules.}
Table~\ref{tab:lrn-transitions} gives the level-changing and
stage-stopping rules. Here $\sigma'$ is the state after the event,
``unchanged'' means the active lesson and level remain exactly as before,
and $\operatorname{AdultAccept}$ is a verified adult action rather than any
child drawing input. Likewise, $\operatorname{AdultSelect}$ is the
authorized action selecting a lesson and its starting level.
A granted extension sets $z=1$ once, with $x=0$; each subsequently
finalized evaluable attempt increments $x$. The extension cannot be
granted again at this stage even if later scores qualify as improvement.
After finalization, a passing result takes priority over stop and
suggestion rules. Otherwise, reaching the base failure limit first
triggers either the single extension grant (if improving and still below
the hard cap) or lesson end. After a grant, exhausting $x=e_p$ or
reaching $n_a=h_p$ ends the lesson. A suggestion is offered only when
none of these higher-priority rules ends the lesson.
For pass-based forward transitions define
$\operatorname{Next}(T)=E$ and $\operatorname{Next}(E)=I$; $I$ has no
successor within its lesson. Passing the last stage ends \emph{that} lesson.

{\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}p{0.22\textwidth}p{0.29\textwidth}p{0.36\textwidth}@{}}
\caption{guided lesson state transitions and authorization}\label{tab:lrn-transitions}\\
\toprule
\textbf{Event} & \textbf{Guard} & \textbf{Required state change or output}\\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Event} & \textbf{Guard} & \textbf{Required state change or output}\\
\midrule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endlastfoot
Adult selects lesson / starting level
($\operatorname{AdultSelect}$) & Supporting adult is authorized
& Set $(\ell,l)$ to adult selection, $q=\textsf{active}$;
reset stage counters, history, suggestion and extension state;
keep any previously persisted lesson record.\\
\addlinespace
Begin practice after demonstration & $q=\textsf{active}$,
$l=D$; child continues after viewing the example
& Set $l=T$ within the same lesson; initialize drawing-stage counters.
This does not start another lesson or require new adult authorization.\\
\addlinespace
Finalize evaluable attempt & $l\in A$, $q=\textsf{active}$
& Classify $C_{\ell,l}(X)$ and $G(l)$; report pass/fail using the
stage threshold; increment $n_a$ and, for non-pass, $n_f,k$.
If $z=1$, increment $x$ for this additional evaluable attempt.
On pass, record the result and apply the prescribed forward-stage or
lesson-end transition below.\\
\addlinespace
Interrupted or invalid input & Missing finalization or
$C_{\ell,l}(X)=\textsf{invalid}$
& Do not pass or change level. Neither $n_a$ nor $n_f$ is incremented, and no successful stage
transition is permitted.\\
\addlinespace
Offer suggestion & $\operatorname{SuggestBack}(\sigma)$
& Set $s=1$, $q=\textsf{suggested}$; leave $\ell,l$ unchanged.\\
\addlinespace
Ignore or decline suggestion & $s=1$
& Clear $s$, reset $k=0$, return $q$ to $\textsf{active}$;
leave $\ell,l$ unchanged and retain the attempt history $H$ and
cumulative stage counts.\\
\addlinespace
Adult accepts suggestion & $s=1$ and an authorized adult
chooses a preceding level
& Activate the chosen earlier level, clear $s$, reset stage counts;
retain the lesson's furthest stage reached.\\
\addlinespace
End at limit & The finalized attempt did not pass and either
$z=0\land n_f\ge b_p\land
(\neg\operatorname{Improving}(H,p)\lor n_a\ge h_p)$,
or $z=1\land(x\ge e_p\lor n_a\ge h_p)$
& Set $q=\textsf{ended}$; report the furthest stage reached for saving;
no more attempts in the ended session. A pass takes priority over this
stop condition.\\
\addlinespace
Extend at base limit & $z=0$, $n_f\ge b_p$,
$\operatorname{Improving}(H,p)$, $n_a<h_p$,
and the finalized attempt did not pass
& Set $z=1$, $x=0$; allow at most $e_p$ additional evaluable attempts,
subject to $n_a<h_p$. Do not regrant this extension or offer a conflicting
return suggestion.\\
\addlinespace
Passing a nonfinal drawing stage & $\operatorname{Pass}(X,l,p)$ and
$l\in\{T,E\}$
& Save the passed attempt at its original stage; automatically set
$l'=\operatorname{Next}(l)$ without adult action. Reset stage-specific
counters and update $f$; leave $\ell$ unchanged.\\
\addlinespace
Passing the final drawing stage & $\operatorname{Pass}(X,I,p)$
& Save the passed independent attempt, set $q'=\textsf{ended}$ and
$f'=I$; complete the current lesson without choosing another lesson.\\
\end{longtable}
}

\paragraph{Invariants and verification obligations.}
For every reachable session state and every adult- or learner-generated
event, the model requires:
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\textbf{I1:}\quad \ell'\ne\ell
  \Longrightarrow\operatorname{AdultSelect};\\
&\textbf{I2:}\quad \text{suggestion ignored or declined}
  \Longrightarrow (\ell',l')=(\ell,l);\\
&\textbf{I3:}\quad C_{\ell,l}(X)\ne\textsf{complete}
  \Longrightarrow \neg\operatorname{Pass}(X,l,p);\\
&\textbf{I4:}\quad \text{pass/feedback never substitutes refined strokes
   for actual guided strokes};\\
&\textbf{I5:}\quad n_a\le h_p\ \land\ 0\le x\le e_p
   \land (z=0\Rightarrow x=0);\\
&\textbf{I6:}\quad \operatorname{Pass}(X,T,p)
  \Longrightarrow (\ell',l')=(\ell,E);\\
&\textbf{I7:}\quad \operatorname{Pass}(X,E,p)
  \Longrightarrow (\ell',l')=(\ell,I);\\
&\textbf{I8:}\quad \operatorname{Pass}(X,I,p)
  \Longrightarrow q'=\textsf{ended}\land\ell'=\ell.
\end{aligned}
\]
Outside ordinary pass-based progression and the demonstration-to-practice
transition, a change to the active stage requires an authorized adult
selection or acceptance (CON-007). Invariants I1 and I6--I8 ensure that
a new lesson is never selected automatically and that passing an attempt
advances only within the current lesson. I2--I5 cover suggestions, invalid
input, faithful records, bounded stage attempts and the one-time extension.
These rules are verified using prepared adult-made attempt sequences
(CON-005--006), including threshold cases with insufficient improvement
history, declined suggestions, a full extension and the hard cap in
Table~\ref{tab:goalconst}.

\subsection{Creative Drawing Requirements}
\label{sec:fr-creative}

This subsection gives the requirements for the creative space, where a
child draws freely beside the structured practice of the guided lessons.
Creative drawing gives room for self-expression, and keeping the drawing
editable keeps the child in control of the work (G.4; SRC-001). The
requirements make G.4 and BR-005 precise: when the child finishes a
drawing, Squigglr refines it automatically and minimally by adjusting the
child's own strokes, the refined drawing stays editable, and the child, or
the adult if the child cannot, can restore the original or turn
refinement off. Nothing in this
subsection applies to guided lessons, whose records keep the child's
actual strokes (BR-004). The requirements serve two product use cases
(UCs): UC-006, in which the child creates and edits artwork, and UC-007,
in which Squigglr refines artwork and the original can be restored. The
team checks every fit criterion on prepared, adult-made drawings, never
with children (CON-005, ASM-014).

The following terms have a fixed meaning in this subsection. A
\emph{creative drawing} is one drawing in the creative space, made of
strokes; each stroke has a path, a colour and a width. A \emph{part} is
one or more strokes that the child selects. A \emph{change} is one stroke
drawn, one editing action, one applied refinement or one restore. The
child \emph{finishes} a drawing by marking it as done; there is no
separate control for asking for refinement. A \emph{refinement} is one
automatic adjustment of the drawing's strokes, and its result is the
\emph{refined drawing}. The \emph{original} of a refinement is the
drawing exactly as it was immediately before that refinement was applied:
the same strokes in the same order, with the same points, colours and
widths. The \emph{supported set} is the agreed set of shapes and simple
objects that Squigglr refines (G.4); its contents are open issue OI-03
(Table~\ref{tab:openIssues}). \texttt{REFINE\_TOLERANCE} is defined in
Table~\ref{tab:goalconst}.

The first group covers what the child can do to a creative drawing,
whether or not it has been refined. These are the editing actions that
the Problem Statement lists as inputs (SRC-001) and that G.4
measurement (b) requires for refined parts.

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-001}{Draw strokes}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall add each stroke the child draws with the stylus in the
creative space to the open creative drawing as a new stroke.}
  \reqrationale{Drawing strokes is the basic input of the creative
space (SRC-001), and the stylus is the main input (CON-003).}
  \reqfit{After an adult tester draws a prepared sequence of
strokes, the drawing holds one new stroke for each stroke drawn, in the
order drawn; each follows the points the browser reported for it
(ASM-004) and can be selected
(\reqref{FR-CRT-002}).}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; CON-003; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-002}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-002}{Select a part}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child select one or more strokes of the open
creative drawing as the part to edit.}
  \reqrationale{Moving, resizing, recolouring and erasing act on a
chosen part of the drawing; the Problem Statement calls them editing
actions on drawing elements (SRC-001). No source names selection, but
every editing action needs it.}
  \reqfit{On a prepared drawing of at least two strokes, the
tester selects one stroke and then a group of strokes; each time, the
selected strokes are visibly marked, and the next editing action changes
exactly the selected strokes.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-003} to \reqref{FR-CRT-006}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-003}{Move a part}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child move a selected part to another place on the
canvas.}
  \reqrationale{Moving is one of the editing actions of G.4
measurement (b) and the Problem Statement (SRC-001).}
  \reqfit{After the tester moves a selected part of a
prepared drawing, every point of the part has moved by the same offset,
in the direction of the movement, and no other stroke has changed.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-002}, \reqref{FR-CRT-013}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-004}{Resize a part}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child make a selected part larger or smaller.}
  \reqrationale{Resizing is one of the editing actions of G.4
measurement (b) and the Problem Statement (SRC-001). Keeping the part's
proportions keeps its shape as the child drew it.}
  \reqfit{After the tester resizes a selected part of a
prepared drawing, the part is a scaled copy of itself: the distance of
each of its points from the centre of the part's bounding box is
multiplied by the same factor, greater than one when enlarged and less
than one when shrunk, and no other stroke has changed.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-002}, \reqref{FR-CRT-013}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-005}{Recolour a part}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child change the colour of a selected part to
another colour that Squigglr offers.}
  \reqrationale{Recolouring is one of the editing actions of G.4
measurement (b) and the Problem Statement (SRC-001).}
  \reqfit{After the tester recolours a selected part of a
prepared drawing, every selected stroke has the chosen colour, its points
are unchanged, and no other stroke has changed.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-002}, \reqref{FR-CRT-013}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-006}{Erase a part}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child erase a selected part.}
  \reqrationale{Erasing is one of the editing actions of G.4
measurement (b) and the Problem Statement (SRC-001).}
  \reqfit{After the tester erases a selected part of a
prepared drawing, the selected strokes are no longer in the drawing and
no other stroke has changed; undoing the erase
(\reqref{FR-CRT-007}) brings them back exactly.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-002}, \reqref{FR-CRT-007}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-007}{Undo a change}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child undo the changes to the open creative
drawing one at a time, most recent first, back to the drawing as it was
when the child opened it.}
  \reqrationale{G.4 measurement (b) requires that refined parts can be
undone, and the Problem Statement lists undoing among the editing actions
(SRC-001). A child who cannot read needs a way back from any mistake,
including an unwanted refinement or restore, without calling an adult
(SRC-005, \intref{P1}{34:13}). Because an applied refinement and a restore
each count as one change, one undo reverses either completely. Changes
made before the drawing was last opened cannot be undone, but the
original stays restorable (\reqref{FR-CRT-024}).}
  \reqfit{The tester makes a prepared sequence of changes to
a prepared drawing, including strokes, each editing action, one
refinement and one restore; after each undo the drawing is exactly as it
was before the corresponding change, and after the last undo it is
exactly as it was when opened.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; \srcref{001}; \srcref{005}, \intref{P1}{34:13}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}, \ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-008}, \reqref{FR-CRT-017}; H-MUH-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-008}{Redo an undone change}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child redo undone changes one at a time until the
child makes a new change.}
  \reqrationale{Without redo, an accidental undo would lose part of the
child's work. No source names redo; it is the usual partner of undo.}
  \reqfit{After the tester undoes a prepared sequence of
changes (\reqref{FR-CRT-007}), each redo returns the drawing
exactly to the state before the matching undo; after any new change, no
redo is available.}
  \reqpriority{Could}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{G.4; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-007}; H-MUH-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-009}{Start a new drawing}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child start a new, empty creative drawing.}
  \reqrationale{A child may want to draw something else; the learner
persona PER-1 wants to finish a picture and show it to his family. The
earlier drawing is kept
(\reqref{FR-CRT-023}). No source names this action.}
  \reqfit{With a saved drawing open, the tester starts a new
drawing; the canvas is empty, and the earlier drawing can be reopened
unchanged (\reqref{FR-CRT-024}).}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-023}, \reqref{FR-CRT-024}}
\end{srsrequirement}

The second group makes ``automatic'' and ``minimal'' in BR-005 testable,
and lets refinement be turned off (UC-007, UC-010).
Unless a fit criterion says otherwise, it is checked on prepared,
adult-made drawings of each item in the supported set.

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-010}{Refine a finished drawing}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall refine the open creative drawing automatically each time
the child finishes it while refinement is turned on, and at no other time.}
  \reqrationale{Refinement is automatic and needs no action from the
child (BR-005), and UC-007 starts when the child finishes a drawing.
Refining only then means that strokes never move while the child is
still drawing, which could confuse a child or take the drawing out of
their hands. Both questionnaire respondents suggested that refinement
be optional or adjustable (SRC-009.01), so \reqref{FR-CRT-030} lets it be
turned off.}
  \reqfit{With refinement turned on, finishing each prepared drawing
starts exactly one refinement; drawing, editing, undoing, restoring and leaving the creative
space without finishing start none.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; \srcref{001}; \srcref{005}, \intref{Moustafa}{6:03}
(as presented by the team)}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-011}, \reqref{FR-CRT-012},
\reqref{FR-CRT-014}, \reqref{FR-CRT-015}, \reqref{FR-CRT-030}; H-MUH-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-011}{Change only stroke paths}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall change only the path of each existing stroke when it
refines a creative drawing.}
  \reqrationale{Refinement must be minimal and the work must remain the
child's own (BR-005). The team presented refinement to the specialists as
tidying the child's lines and curves, not swapping in a different picture
(SRC-005, \intref{Moustafa}{6:03}); the interview specialists did not
comment on it (FACT-013), but the questionnaire respondents asked that
refinement stay subtle and support the child's own drawing rather than
replace or correct it (SRC-009.01). Adding, removing or replacing strokes,
or changing their colours or widths, would make the drawing less the
child's own.}
  \reqfit{For each prepared drawing, the refined drawing has
the same number of strokes as its original, in the same order, and each
refined stroke has the colour and width of the corresponding original
stroke.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; \srcref{005}, \intref{Moustafa}{6:03} (as
presented by the team); \hyperref[src:009.01]{SRC-009.01}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-012}, \reqref{FR-CRT-020}; H-MUH-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{sloppypar}
\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-012}{Stay within the refinement tolerance}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall keep every refined stroke within
\texttt{REFINE\_\allowbreak TOLERANCE} of the corresponding original stroke.}
  \reqrationale{This makes G.4 measurement (a) precise. The check runs
both ways, so a refinement that shortens a stroke or drops part of it
also fails: points of the original would then lie far from the refined
stroke. The team sets \texttt{REFINE\_\allowbreak TOLERANCE} with adult-made
drawings (BR-009).}
  \reqfit{For each prepared drawing, every point of each
refined stroke lies within \texttt{REFINE\_\allowbreak TOLERANCE} of the
corresponding original stroke, and every point of each original stroke
lies within \texttt{REFINE\_\allowbreak TOLERANCE} of the corresponding refined
stroke.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; BR-009; ASM-014}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-011}, \reqref{FR-CRT-020}; H-MUH-002, H-MUH-006}
\end{srsrequirement}
\end{sloppypar}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-013}{Keep the refined drawing editable}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child edit each stroke of a refined drawing with
the same actions, and with the same results, as a stroke the child
drew.}
  \reqrationale{G.4 measurement (b) requires that refined parts can be
moved, resized, recoloured, erased and undone. A refined drawing returned
only as a picture could not be edited, which the Development Plan already
rules out (SRC-002).}
  \reqfit{For each prepared refined drawing, each of
\reqref{FR-CRT-002} to \reqref{FR-CRT-007}
applied to refined strokes meets its own fit criterion.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; \srcref{001}; \srcref{002}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}, \ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-002} to \reqref{FR-CRT-007}; H-MUH-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-014}{Do not refine a stroke twice}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall not refine again a stroke that an earlier refinement
changed, unless the child has edited that stroke since.}
  \reqrationale{Each refinement stays within \texttt{REFINE\_\allowbreak TOLERANCE}
of its own input, so refining the same strokes every time the child
finishes would let the drawing drift further and further from what the
child drew. A child may finish the same drawing many times. No source
states this case.}
  \reqfit{Finishing a refined drawing again without any
change leaves it exactly as it was; after the tester edits one refined
stroke and draws one new stroke, finishing again changes no stroke other
than those two.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-010}, \reqref{FR-CRT-012}; H-MUH-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-015}{Leave unsupported drawings unchanged}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall leave a finished creative drawing unchanged if it has no
strokes or is not a drawing of an item in the supported set.}
  \reqrationale{G.4 commits to refining drawings of items in the agreed
supported set only, and the Development Plan keeps refinement of
arbitrary drawings out of the proof of concept (SRC-002). The team
presented a version that would still tidy the lines of a drawing it could
not identify (SRC-005, \intref{Moustafa}{6:03}); until the supported set
is agreed (OI-03), leaving such drawings as the child made them is the
choice that protects the child's work.}
  \reqfit{Finishing an empty canvas, and finishing each
drawing in a prepared set of adult-made drawings of items outside the
supported set, leaves each drawing exactly as it was and editable.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; \srcref{002}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-010}; H-MUH-002, H-MUH-006}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-016}{Show whether a drawing is refined}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall show, while a creative drawing is open, whether it holds a
refinement that has not been undone or restored.}
  \reqrationale{The child and the supporting adult need to tell a
refined drawing from the child's original; otherwise an adult cannot see
what Squigglr changed, and the child cannot see that restoring is
possible. How the state is shown to a child who cannot read is a
usability matter.}
  \reqfit{For prepared drawings in each of these states (never
refined; refined; refined then edited; refined then restored; refinement
undone; refinement failed), what Squigglr shows matches the state in
every case.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-017}; H-MUH-003, H-MUH-006}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-030}{Turn refinement off}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child, and the supporting adult, turn
automatic refinement off and on for a learner profile.}
  \reqrationale{UC-007, UC-010 and the data flows F2 and F9 let refinement be
turned off, and both questionnaire respondents suggested that refinement be
optional or adjustable (SRC-009.01). Whether children value automatic
refinement is not verified (ASM-011); a child who does not accept refined
drawings as their own needs a way to keep drawings unrefined (G.4).}
  \reqfit{After the tester, once as the child in the creative space and
once as the adult in the learner's settings, turns refinement off,
finishing a prepared drawing of the supported set leaves its strokes
exactly as drawn; after refinement is turned on again, finishing the
drawing refines it.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; ASM-011; \hyperref[src:009.01]{SRC-009.01}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \ucref{010}; \reqref{FR-CRT-010}; H-MUH-006}
\end{srsrequirement}

The third group covers restoring the original. BR-005 lets the child, or
the adult if the child cannot, restore it. \reqref{FR-CRT-017} defines what a
restore returns, and \reqref{FR-CRT-018} and
\reqref{FR-CRT-019} say who can restore.

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-017}{Restore the exact original}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall return a creative drawing, on restore, exactly to the
original of its most recent refinement that has not been undone or
restored.}
  \reqrationale{This makes G.4 measurement (c) precise. After one
refinement, a restore returns the child's drawing as it was before
refinement. After several, each restore steps back one refinement, so no
earlier version of the child's work is lost. A restore also removes edits
made after that refinement; because a restore is one change, the child
can undo it (\reqref{FR-CRT-007}) to get the refined and
edited drawing back.}
  \reqfit{For each prepared drawing: (a) after one refinement
and a restore, the drawing equals its original stroke for stroke and
point for point, in the same colours and widths; (b) after a refinement,
an edit, a restore and an undo, the drawing equals the refined and edited
drawing exactly; (c) after a refinement, new strokes, a second
refinement and two restores, the drawing equals the original of the
second refinement after the first restore and the original of the first
refinement after the second; (d) no restore is offered when no
refinement remains that has not been undone or restored.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-007}, \reqref{FR-CRT-018},
\reqref{FR-CRT-019}; H-MUH-001, H-MUH-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-018}{Child restores the original}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child restore the original of the open creative
drawing.}
  \reqrationale{BR-005 lets the child restore the original, which keeps
the child in control of the work (G.4). The team presented this to the
specialists as part of the creative space (SRC-005,
\intref{Moustafa}{6:03}). A child who cannot read must be able to find
the control, which is a usability matter.}
  \reqfit{From the child's view of each prepared refined
drawing, the tester restores the original with one action, and the result
meets \reqref{FR-CRT-017}.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; \srcref{001}; \srcref{005}, \intref{Moustafa}{6:03}
(as presented by the team)}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-017}; H-MUH-001, H-MUH-006}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-019}{Adult restores the original}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the supporting adult, after passing the adult check of
SAF-RAY-001, restore the original of any creative drawing saved under a
learner profile in that adult's account.}
  \reqrationale{BR-005 lets the adult restore the original for a child
who cannot, and BR-001 limits a learner's work to the adult who holds the
account. The adult may need to do this when the child is not using the
drawing, for example after a session. The adult check keeps a child from
reaching this adult action by tapping icons (SAF-RAY-001).}
  \reqfit{The tester, signed in as the adult of a prepared
account and after passing the adult check of SAF-RAY-001, restores the
original of a saved refined drawing; reopening the
drawing under its learner profile shows a result that meets
\reqref{FR-CRT-017}.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-001; BR-005}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-017}, \reqref{FR-CRT-023},
SAF-RAY-001; H-MUH-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

The fourth group covers failure and interruption. A refinement may fail,
return an unusable result, or finish after the child has moved on, for
example when the connection is slow or lost (ASM-005). How quickly
refinement must respond, and how Squigglr copes when refinement is
unavailable, belong to the performance requirements
(Section~\ref{sec:performance}).

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-020}{Leave the drawing unchanged when refinement fails}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall make no change to a creative drawing when its refinement
fails or the refinement result violates
\reqref{FR-CRT-011} or \reqref{FR-CRT-012}.}
  \reqrationale{This restates G.4 measurement (d) for automatic
refinement, which the child never requests (BR-005). A partly applied or
invalid result could change the drawing more than minimally, so it is
applied whole or not at all. The Development Plan also requires that an
invalid result leave the drawing unchanged (SRC-002). The drawing stays
editable, and finishing it again starts a new refinement
(\reqref{FR-CRT-010}).}
  \reqfit{For each prepared drawing and each of these
simulated cases (lost connection; an error during refinement; no result
within the time limit of the speed requirements; a result that adds a
stroke; a result with a point outside \texttt{REFINE\_\allowbreak TOLERANCE}; a
malformed result), the drawing afterwards equals the drawing as finished,
can be edited (\reqref{FR-CRT-002} to
\reqref{FR-CRT-007}) and, from PH-2, when
\reqref{FR-CRT-016} applies, is shown as not refined.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; ASM-005; \srcref{002}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-011}, \reqref{FR-CRT-012}; H-MUH-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-021}{Discard an out-of-date result}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall discard a refinement result if the drawing has been
changed or closed since that refinement started.}
  \reqrationale{A result computed from an older version of the drawing
would remove the child's newer strokes, or change a drawing the child
has left. Discarding it keeps the drawing as the child made it; the child
can finish again. No source states this case.}
  \reqfit{For each prepared drawing, with the result delayed
by the test set-up: (a) if the tester draws a stroke before the result
arrives, the drawing afterwards is the finished drawing plus that stroke,
unrefined; (b) if the tester leaves the creative space before the result
arrives, reopening the drawing shows it exactly as left, unrefined.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005; ASM-005}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}, \ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-022}, \reqref{FR-CRT-024}; H-MUH-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-022}{Keep drawing while refinement runs}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the child keep drawing and editing while a refinement
is in progress.}
  \reqrationale{A child kept waiting may lose focus (PER-1), and
children should be able to use Squigglr without constant adult help
(SRC-005, \intref{P1}{34:13}). No source states this case.}
  \reqfit{With the result delayed by the test set-up, the
tester draws a stroke and moves a part before the result arrives; both
changes meet \reqref{FR-CRT-001} and
\reqref{FR-CRT-003}, and
\reqref{FR-CRT-021} then applies.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; \srcref{005}, \intref{P1}{34:13}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}, \ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-021}}
\end{srsrequirement}

The fifth group covers saving and reopening. UC-006 ends with the drawing
saved and still editable. One account can hold several learner profiles
and a device may be shared by several children (BR-001; SRC-004), so each
creative drawing belongs to one learner profile.

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-023}{Save to the active learner}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall save each creative drawing, with the originals of its
refinements, to the learner profile that was active when the child drew
it, without any action from the child.}
  \reqrationale{A child who cannot read may leave without saving.
Restoring after reopening needs the originals
(\reqref{FR-CRT-024}). Saving to the wrong learner would
show one child's work as another's (BR-001).}
  \reqfit{For two learner profiles in one prepared account,
the tester draws, refines and edits a drawing under each and leaves the
creative space with its own controls; reopening each learner's creative
space shows that learner's drawing exactly as left, and not the other
learner's.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-001; BR-005}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-024}, \reqref{FR-CRT-026}; H-MUH-003, H-MUH-004, H-MUH-005}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-024}{Reopen a drawing as it was left}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall reopen a saved creative drawing exactly as it was last
saved, with every stroke editable and every refinement that has not been
undone or restored still restorable.}
  \reqrationale{UC-006 requires the drawing to stay editable, and
BR-005 requires the original to stay restorable; neither ends when the
child leaves the creative space.}
  \reqfit{For prepared drawings saved in each state listed in
\reqref{FR-CRT-016}, reopening gives the same strokes and
the same refined state, every stroke can be edited
(\reqref{FR-CRT-013}), and a restore meets
\reqref{FR-CRT-017}.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; BR-005}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}, \ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-017}, \reqref{FR-CRT-023}; H-MUH-001, H-MUH-005}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-025}{Keep changes through a lost connection}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall save, once the connection returns, every change the child
made to an open creative drawing while the connection was lost.}
  \reqrationale{The connection may drop during normal use, and offline
use is out of scope (ASM-005), so this covers a drawing that stays open
until the connection returns. Reporting a save that fails is a
robustness requirement.}
  \reqfit{With a prepared drawing open, the test set-up cuts
the connection; the tester draws strokes and edits a part, the connection
is restored, and the tester leaves the creative space; reopening the
drawing shows every change.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{G.4; ASM-005}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-023}; H-MUH-005}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-029}{Show unsaved changes}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall show, while a creative drawing is open, whether every change
to it has been saved.}
  \reqrationale{Offline use is out of scope (ASM-005), so changes made
while the connection is lost are kept only while the page stays open
(\reqref{FR-CRT-025}); an adult who can see that changes are
not yet saved can keep the page open until they are. How the state is
shown to a child who cannot read is a usability requirement.}
  \reqfit{With a prepared drawing open, the tester edits it
with the connection cut; the display shows unsaved changes; after the
connection returns and the save completes, it shows that all changes are
saved.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{G.4; ASM-005; CA-003}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-023}, \reqref{FR-CRT-025}; H-MUH-005}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-026}{Show only the active learner's drawings}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall show in the creative space only the creative drawings
saved under the active learner profile.}
  \reqrationale{Several children may use one device and one account
(SRC-004; BR-001); showing another child's drawing would expose their work
and let it be changed by someone else. An adult selects the active
learner (ASM-008).}
  \reqfit{With drawings saved under two learner profiles of
one prepared account, the creative space of each learner shows only that
learner's drawings.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{BR-001; ASM-008; \srcref{004}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}; \reqref{FR-CRT-023}; H-MUH-004}
\end{srsrequirement}

The last group keeps the creative space apart from the guided-lesson
records that adults rely on (BR-004; ULC-005 in
Table~\ref{tab:unlikelyChanges}).

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-027}{No refinement in guided lessons}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall not refine any stroke drawn in a guided lesson.}
  \reqrationale{BR-005 limits refinement to the creative space, and a
guided-lesson record keeps the child's actual strokes (BR-004). A refined
stroke in a lesson would show adults work the child did not do. How the
record keeps those strokes is specified in
Section~\ref{sec:fr-records}.}
  \reqfit{For prepared attempts at each drawing stage
(full-outline tracing, endpoint-only guidance and independent drawing) of
each catalogue lesson, no refinement starts, and the strokes shown during and
after each attempt are point for point the strokes drawn.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; G.5; BR-004; BR-005; \srcref{005},
\intref{Moustafa}{6:03} (as presented by the team)}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}, \ucref{007}; \reqref{FR-CRT-010}; H-MUH-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-CRT-028}{Creative drawings are not attempts}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall not count a creative drawing as an attempt.}
  \reqrationale{Attempt records and progress summaries describe guided
practice (G.5, G.6). A refined creative drawing counted as an attempt
would make assisted work look like the child's own.}
  \reqfit{For a prepared learner profile with saved attempts,
drawing, refining and restoring prepared creative drawings leaves every
attempt record, lesson record and progress summary of that learner
exactly as before.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.4; G.5; G.6; BR-004}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{006}, \ucref{008}, \ucref{009}; \reqref{FR-CRT-023}; H-MUH-003}
\end{srsrequirement}


\subsection{Attempt Record Requirements}
\label{sec:fr-records}

This subsection specifies what Squigglr keeps about guided practice, so
that a supporting adult can see how a child practised and not only what
the final drawing looks like (G.5). The requirements serve use cases
UC-008 (record an attempt) and UC-003 (practise a guided lesson). An
\emph{attempt} is one try at one stage of a lesson. A stage (also
called a guidance level in Table~\ref{tab:glossary}) is one of the stages of decreasing
help listed in the Goals of the Project subsection; attempts are made at
the drawing stages: tracing a full outline, drawing between endpoints
only, and drawing with no guide. Squigglr keeps an \emph{attempt record}
for each attempt and a \emph{lesson record} each time a learner starts a
lesson. Creative drawings are not attempts (FR-CRT-028); the Creative
Drawing Requirements subsection covers them.

Three safety requirements in the Safety-Critical Requirements subsection
already govern records, and the requirements below do not repeat them.
SAF-RAY-003 keeps each completed attempt on the device until the server
confirms that it is saved, and tells the adult which attempts were not
saved. SAF-RAY-006 records each lesson that ends at an attempt limit,
either \texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT} or \texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT\_MAX}, records
each improvement extension, and keeps earlier attempts when a lesson is
restarted. SAF-RAY-007 saves every attempt to the active learner only.
The specialist interview did not cover how records are kept, shared or
deleted (FACT-013), so the requirements below rest on the Problem
Statement and the business rules. Who may view, share or delete records
is decided in the Security Requirements section (Open Issue OI-02,
Table~\ref{tab:openIssues}). How a lesson moves between stages, and when
an attempt ends, belong to the Learning Activity Requirements. Phases
are the delivery phases PH-1, PH-2 and PH-3 defined, with their dates,
in the Introduction.

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-REC-001}{Attempt record}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall keep, for each attempt that ends with an outcome, an
attempt record holding the lesson, the stage, the attempt's result, the
outcome (whether the attempt passed its stage, FR-LRN-013) and the date
and time the attempt ended.}
  \reqrationale{A finished drawing does not show how much guidance the
child had (SRC-001). The stage tells the adult whether the attempt was
made with a guide or without one, and the date and time place the
attempt in the learner's history for progress summaries (G.6). The
learner is fixed by SAF-RAY-007. The result is kept because the
improvement rule compares the results of recent attempts (BR-002,
FR-LRN-020), and the adult sees each attempt's result (ASM-006).}
  \reqfit{For a set of prepared, adult-made attempts with
known lessons, stages, results and outcomes, including at least one
passing and
one failing attempt at each drawing stage, every saved attempt record
shows exactly the known lesson, stage, result and outcome, and the
records are in
the order the attempts ended.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.2, G.5; BR-002; \srcref{001}; \srcref{002} (PoC plan, adaptive
guidance experiment)}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}, \ucref{008}; SAF-RAY-007,
FR-LRN-013, FR-LRN-020, FR-PRG-002, FR-PRG-003; H-MUH-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-REC-002}{Actual strokes}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall keep in each attempt record the strokes the child drew in
the attempt, in the order drawn, as they were captured.}
  \reqrationale{Adults judge progress from the records, and a record
that shows a corrected or ideal shape would mislead them (BR-004). This
principle was presented to the domain specialists in the interview
(SRC-005, \intref{Moustafa}{6:03}). Duplicate or unmappable input is
handled by SAF-AHM-001 and SAF-AHM-002, so the strokes kept are those the
child drew. Refinement never reaches guided-lesson strokes (BR-005;
FR-CRT-027).}
  \reqfit{For prepared, adult-made attempts whose strokes are
captured by the test harness as drawn, every saved attempt record holds
the same strokes, point for point and in the same order, and no attempt
record holds a stroke that was not drawn in that attempt.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.5; BR-004; ASM-004; \srcref{001}; \srcref{005},
\intref{Moustafa}{6:03}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}, \ucref{008}; FR-LRN-007,
SAF-AHM-001, SAF-AHM-002, FR-PRG-006; H-MUH-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-REC-003}{Difficulties and corrections}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall keep in each attempt record every predefined difficulty
observed during the attempt, each marked as corrected or not corrected
within that attempt.}
  \reqrationale{Feedback lets the child go back and complete a missed
part within the same attempt (BR-007). If the correction erased the
difficulty, the record would hide what the child found hard; G.5
requires that no difficulty is lost after a correction. The team
presented records that keep both the miss and its later completion
(SRC-005, \intref{Moustafa}{6:03}).}
  \reqfit{For prepared attempts with known predefined
difficulties, some corrected within the attempt and some not, every
saved attempt record lists every known difficulty once, with the correct
mark, and lists no other difficulty.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{G.5; BR-007; \srcref{001}; \srcref{002} (PoC plan, adaptive
guidance experiment); \srcref{005}, \intref{Moustafa}{6:03}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}, \ucref{008}; FR-PRG-004,
FR-PRG-006; H-MUH-007}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-REC-004}{Adult-set values in use}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall keep in each attempt record the value of each adult-set
value that was in use for the learner when the attempt was made.}
  \reqrationale{The adult-set values are the six values of BR-008
listed in Table~\ref{tab:goalconst}, and the outcome of an attempt depends on the
\texttt{STAGE\_\allowbreak PASS} in use, and the adult may change the values at any
time (BR-008). Keeping the values with each attempt means a later change
does not alter what an earlier record means, and lets the adult see every
value Squigglr records (ASM-006, LC-007). SAF-RAY-008 shows the values
currently in use; this requirement keeps those in use for each
attempt.}
  \reqfit{For prepared attempts made before and after an
adult changes each adult-set value, each
attempt record holds the values in use when that attempt was made.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.5; BR-008; ASM-006; LC-007; \srcref{008}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{008}, \ucref{010}; SAF-RAY-008,
FR-PRG-006; H-MUH-007}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-REC-005}{Interrupted attempt}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall keep an attempt that an adult stops in Squigglr, by
ending the session or switching learner, after at least one stroke but
before its outcome is decided, as an attempt record marked as
interrupted, holding the strokes and predefined difficulties up to that
point and no outcome.}
  \reqrationale{An adult may stop a session (ASM-015) or switch
learners on a shared device (ASM-008) in the middle of an attempt. The
strokes drawn are still practice the adult may want to see, but the
attempt has no outcome, so it must not appear as passed or failed.
An interrupted attempt does not consume an evaluable attempt from the
stage limit (FR-LRN-009). An attempt cut off by closing
the page or losing the connection may not reach Squigglr; completed
attempts are protected by SAF-RAY-003.}
  \reqfit{For prepared attempts stopped by an adult action
after one or more strokes, every saved attempt record is marked as
interrupted, holds the strokes drawn and has no outcome; an attempt
stopped before any stroke leaves no attempt record.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.5; ASM-008, ASM-015; CA-003, CA-004}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}, \ucref{008}; FR-LRN-009,
FR-PRG-009; H-MUH-004}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-REC-006}{Lesson record}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall keep, each time a learner starts a lesson, a lesson record
holding the lesson, the starting stage, the furthest stage reached, the
number of successful attempts at each stage and how the lesson ended.}
  \reqrationale{Caregivers and educators need to see how far the child
got in each lesson and how many attempts succeeded at each stage
(Table~\ref{tab:userNeeds}; BR-002). The starting stage shows whether a
stage was reached by passing or chosen by an adult (BR-003). A new record
each time the lesson starts keeps a restart from hiding earlier results
(SAF-RAY-006).}
  \reqfit{For prepared sequences of attempts in which a
lesson is started at the first drawing stage and again at a later stage
chosen by an adult, each lesson record shows the known starting stage,
furthest stage, count of successful attempts at each stage and ending.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.5; BR-002, BR-003; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}, \ucref{004}, \ucref{008}; SAF-RAY-006, FR-PRG-002, FR-PRG-010; H-MUH-007}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-REC-007}{Unfinished lesson}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall keep the lesson record of a lesson that stops before its
last stage is passed and before it reaches an attempt limit, marked as
unfinished, with the furthest stage reached and the successful attempts
at each stage up to that point.}
  \reqrationale{BR-002 saves progress when a lesson ends at
\texttt{ATTEMPT\_\allowbreak LIMIT}, and SAF-RAY-006 records those endings. A lesson
an adult stops early (ASM-015) would otherwise leave no record of the
stages already passed.}
  \reqfit{For prepared lessons stopped by an adult after
known successful attempts, each lesson record is marked as unfinished and
shows the known furthest stage and counts; for prepared lessons whose
last stage is passed, no lesson record is marked as unfinished.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.5; BR-002; ASM-015}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}, \ucref{008}; SAF-RAY-006,
FR-PRG-002; H-MUH-007}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-REC-008}{Each attempt saved once}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall save each attempt record exactly once, under the lesson
record of the lesson in which the attempt was made.}
  \reqrationale{A save that is retried after the connection drops
(CA-003, SAF-RAY-003) could store an attempt twice or under another
lesson, and two sessions open for the same learner could mix their
attempts. Either would change the counts an adult relies on.}
  \reqfit{For prepared attempts saved with the connection cut
and restored during each save, and for prepared attempts made for one
learner in two sessions open at the same time, every attempt appears in
exactly one attempt record, under the lesson record in which it was made,
and the counts in each lesson record equal the known counts.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.5; ASM-005; CA-003, CA-004}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{008}; SAF-RAY-003, SAF-RAY-007; H-MUH-004, H-RAY-002, H-RAY-006}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-REC-009}{Saved records unchanged}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall keep the contents of every saved attempt record, and of
every lesson record whose lesson has ended, unchanged except by
deletion.}
  \reqrationale{Later events, such as an adult changing an adult-set
value, a starting stage or the lesson catalogue (LC-001, LC-002), must
not rewrite what an earlier record says, or an attempt made with a guide
could later appear to have been made without one. Deletion is decided
with the privacy requirements (OI-02).}
  \reqfit{For a prepared history, after the adult changes
every adult-set value, the starting stage of a lesson and the learner's
other settings, and after a lesson is added to or removed from the
catalogue, every saved attempt record and every lesson record of an ended
lesson is identical to its contents before those changes.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.5; BR-004, BR-008; LC-001, LC-002}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{008}, \ucref{010}; FR-REC-004; H-MUH-003, H-MUH-004, H-MUH-007}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-REC-010}{Suggestion responses}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall keep in the lesson record each suggestion to go
back a stage that it offers and the response of the adult to it.}
  \reqrationale{A go-back suggestion changes the stage only when an adult
accepts it (BR-003). Keeping the suggestion and the response shows an
adult why a later attempt was made at an earlier stage, and keeps a
declined suggestion visible (SAF-RAY-001).}
  \reqfit{For prepared sequences of attempts in which suggestions are
accepted, declined and not yet answered, every lesson record lists each
suggestion once, with the known response, and lists no other suggestion.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.3; BR-003}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{005}; \ucref{008}; FR-LRN-019; SAF-RAY-001, SAF-RAY-007}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Progress Summary Requirements}
\label{sec:fr-progress}

This subsection specifies the progress summary: the view in which a
supporting adult reviews a learner's practice over time and opens the
attempt records behind it (G.6). The requirements serve use cases UC-009
(review progress) and UC-010 (review history and change settings). A
progress summary is built only from the attempt and lesson records of the
Attempt Record Requirements (Section~\ref{sec:fr-records}). It is meant for
an adult such as PER-2, who reviews a child's history once a week to see
which stages he reached and what keeps going wrong, and PER-3, who works
with several learners a day. Attempts \emph{made with a guide} are those
at the tracing and endpoint stages; attempts \emph{made without a guide}
are those at the stage with no guide. FR-LRN-012 calls these attempts
assisted and independent.

The requirements below leave three matters to other requirements.
SAF-RAY-004 forbids diagnostic labels, judgements of the child's
abilities and comparisons with other children in any record or summary,
and requires each summary to state that it describes practice only.
SAF-RAY-006 shows how many times each lesson ended at a limit or was
extended. Access to summaries is limited to the account-holding adult
(BR-001, ULC-002) by the Security Requirements section, which also
decides whether summaries can be shared or exported (OI-02).

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-001}{One learner per summary}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall show in each progress summary only the records of the one
learner profile that the summary names.}
  \reqrationale{One account can hold several learner profiles (BR-001),
and an educator must never show one learner's records to another
(PER-3). Naming the learner lets the adult check whose records are
shown.}
  \reqfit{For a prepared account with at least three learner
profiles, each with a known history, every summary names one learner and
contains every record of that learner and no record of another.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.6; BR-001; ASM-008; \srcref{004}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}, \ucref{010}; SAF-RAY-007; H-MUH-004, H-RAY-006}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-002}{Progress over time by lesson}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall show, for each lesson in a progress summary, the learner's
lesson records in the order they were started, each with its starting
stage, furthest stage reached, successful attempts at each stage and how
the lesson ended.}
  \reqrationale{Caregivers and educators need the furthest stage and
the successful attempts at each stage (Table~\ref{tab:userNeeds}), and
the order shows change over time (G.6). How the lesson ended (every
stage passed, ended at a limit, or unfinished) keeps an unfinished
lesson from looking like a lesson the child could not pass.}
  \reqfit{For prepared histories showing improvement, a lesson
ended at \texttt{ATTEMPT\_\allowbreak LIMIT} and an unfinished lesson, every summary
lists the lesson records in start order with the known values and
endings.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.6; BR-002; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}; FR-REC-001, FR-REC-006,
FR-REC-007, SAF-RAY-006; H-MUH-008}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-003}{With and without a guide kept apart}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall show attempts made without a guide separately from
attempts made with a guide in every part of a progress summary that
counts or lists attempts.}
  \reqrationale{The adults need to see changes in independent drawing,
which a combined figure would hide (SRC-001; G.6). Assisted work that
appears independent could lead an adult to move a child on too soon.}
  \reqfit{For prepared histories with known numbers of
attempts made with and without a guide, every count or list in the
summary shows the two kinds separately and correctly, and no figure adds
them together; at least \texttt{ADULT\_\allowbreak REVIEWERS} adult reviewers state
which attempts were made without a guide, with at least
\texttt{REVIEW\_\allowbreak PASS\_\allowbreak RATE} of their tasks succeeding.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.2, G.6; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}; FR-REC-001, FR-LRN-012; H-MUH-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-004}{Recurring difficulties}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall list in each progress summary every predefined difficulty
observed in at least \texttt{RECUR\_\allowbreak MIN} of the learner's attempts at the
same stage of the same lesson, with the number of those attempts and the
number in which the child corrected it.}
  \reqrationale{Adults want to know what keeps going wrong (PER-2), and
the Problem Statement promises summaries that show recurring
difficulties (SRC-001). A fixed count makes ``recurring'' testable. The
constant \texttt{RECUR\_\allowbreak MIN} is defined in Table~\ref{tab:goalconst}.
Counting corrections shows difficulties that are being
resolved as well as those that keep coming up (SRC-005,
\intref{Moustafa}{6:03}).}
  \reqfit{For a prepared history in which one predefined
difficulty occurs in exactly \texttt{RECUR\_\allowbreak MIN} attempts at one stage
and another in one attempt fewer, the summary lists the first, with the
known counts, and not the second.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.6; BR-007; \srcref{001}; \srcref{005},
\intref{Moustafa}{6:03}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}; FR-REC-003; H-MUH-008}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-005}{Open the attempts behind the summary}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the adult open, from each figure or item in a
progress summary, the attempt records from which it was computed.}
  \reqrationale{Adults need to inspect the attempts behind a summary
(G.6), and seeing the drawings lets them judge for themselves rather than
rely on a number.}
  \reqfit{For prepared histories, every figure or item opens
exactly the attempt records it was computed from; at least
\texttt{ADULT\_\allowbreak REVIEWERS} adult reviewers locate the attempts behind given
items, with at least \texttt{REVIEW\_\allowbreak PASS\_\allowbreak RATE} of their tasks
succeeding.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.6; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}; FR-PRG-006; H-MUH-008}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-006}{Attempt record view}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall show, for each attempt record the adult opens, every value
kept in it under FR-REC-001 to FR-REC-005, with the strokes drawn as
recorded.}
  \reqrationale{Squigglr shows the adult every value it records
(ASM-006), and the strokes show what the child actually drew
(BR-004).}
  \reqfit{For each prepared attempt record, the view shows
its lesson, stage, outcome or interrupted mark, date and time,
predefined difficulties with their correction marks, adult-set values in
use, and strokes identical to those recorded.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.5, G.6; BR-004; ASM-006}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}, \ucref{010}; FR-REC-001 to
FR-REC-005; H-MUH-008}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-007}{Adult chooses the view}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall let the adult choose which lessons, dates and recorded
values a progress summary shows.}
  \reqrationale{Which values each adult wants to see will differ and
change (LC-007), and the specialists advised that useful progress
measures be chosen with occupational therapists or educators
(\hyperref[src:005.03]{SRC-005.03}), so the adult chooses rather than the team (ASM-006).
Until the adult chooses, the summary covers all of the learner's
records.}
  \reqfit{For a prepared history, each choice of lessons,
dates and values changes only what the summary shows: the records are
unchanged, and attempts made with and without a guide stay separate.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{G.6; ASM-006; LC-007; \hyperref[src:005.03]{SRC-005.03}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}; FR-PRG-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-008}{Empty history}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall show, when the learner has no saved attempt record in the
chosen lessons and dates, a progress summary that states that no
practice is recorded and shows no stage, count or difficulty.}
  \reqrationale{A new learner has no history, and zeros or empty
stages could be read as failure.}
  \reqfit{For a new learner profile, and for a learner whose
records all lie outside the chosen dates, the summary states that no
practice is recorded and shows no stage, count or difficulty.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.6; BR-006}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}; FR-PRG-007; H-MUH-008, H-RAY-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-009}{Interrupted attempts shown apart}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall show interrupted attempts in a progress summary
separately from successful and unsuccessful attempts.}
  \reqrationale{An interrupted attempt has no outcome (FR-REC-005);
counting it as failed would understate the child's practice, and
counting it as passed would overstate it.}
  \reqfit{For a prepared learner whose only attempts are
interrupted, the summary shows them as interrupted and shows no
successful or unsuccessful attempt and no stage passed; adding prepared
interrupted attempts to any history leaves its successful and
unsuccessful counts unchanged.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.6; ASM-015}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}; FR-REC-005; H-MUH-008}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-010}{Stages not attempted}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall mark each stage of a lesson that the learner has not
attempted, including a stage before an adult-chosen starting stage, as
not attempted, distinct from a stage attempted without success.}
  \reqrationale{An adult may start the child at a later stage (BR-003),
and a learner with a short history has attempted only some stages. A
stage left blank or shown as zero successes could be read as a stage the
child could not draw.}
  \reqfit{For a prepared history with a lesson started at a
later stage and one stage attempted only without success, the summary
marks the skipped stages as not attempted and shows the attempted stage
with zero successful attempts.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.6; BR-003; \srcref{001}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{004}, \ucref{009}; FR-REC-006; H-MUH-003, H-MUH-008}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-011}{Latest attempt included}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall state on each progress summary the date and time of the
most recent attempt it includes.}
  \reqrationale{Attempts still held on the child's device
(SAF-RAY-003) are not in a summary that an adult opens on another
device. The date of the latest attempt lets the adult notice recent
practice that is missing.}
  \reqfit{For a prepared history with attempts saved and
attempts held back by a cut connection, the summary shows the date and
time of the latest saved attempt and includes none of the held-back
attempts.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.6; ASM-005; CA-003}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}; SAF-RAY-003; H-MUH-008, H-RAY-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{FR-PRG-012}{No overall score}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall show no figure in a progress summary that combines the
results of more than one stage into a single score, grade or rating.}
  \reqrationale{The needs of caregivers and educators exclude a single
overall score (Table~\ref{tab:userNeeds}; PER-2), and the team cannot
judge a child's abilities (BR-006; SRC-008). Results per stage and per
lesson answer the adults' questions without a score.}
  \reqfit{Inspection of the summaries for prepared histories
finds no score, grade, rating or percentage computed from attempts at
more than one stage.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{G.6; BR-006; \srcref{001}, \srcref{008}; \srcref{005},
\intref{Moustafa}{6:03}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}; SAF-RAY-004; H-RAY-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\section{Look and Feel Requirements}
\label{sec:look-feel}
% Long constant names cannot be hyphenated; let lines stretch rather than
% overflow the margin in this section.
\begingroup
\sloppy

This section states how Squigglr looks (Section~\ref{sec:appearance}) and
the mood its style creates (Section~\ref{sec:style}). A
\emph{child-facing screen} is any screen the child uses in a guided lesson
or the creative space; an \emph{adult view} is any screen for setup,
settings, records or progress summaries. No requirement is gathered from
children and no study with children takes place (CON-005, CON-006), so
the fit criteria are checked by inspecting every screen or by adult
reviewers. An adult review shows that adults find a design suitable, not
that children can use it; checking that needs a study with ethics
approval, which is out of scope (\srcref{005.11}).

The specialists advised starting from the Web Content Accessibility
Guidelines (WCAG) 2.2~\cite{wcag22} (\srcref{005.08}), and the supervisor
advised taking specific WCAG ideas as non-functional requirements rather
than treating WCAG as a constraint (\srcref{008}). Each requirement below
that is based on a WCAG success criterion names it in its rationale.
Further WCAG-based accessibility criteria are in
Section~\ref{sec:usability}, and listing WCAG as a standard is open issue
OI-08.

\subsection{Appearance Requirements}
\label{sec:appearance}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-001}{Calm, uncluttered child screens}
  \reqshall{Each child-facing screen shall offer the child no more than
\texttt{CHILD\_CONTROLS\_MAX} controls and shall show no decorative
animation or flashing while the child is drawing.}
  \reqrationale{Too many visual or auditory stimuli can overwhelm children,
and specialists advised a calm, minimally stimulating interface
(\srcref{009.05}). A child who cannot read navigates by images, so every
extra control is something more to interpret (\srcref{005.05}). Banning
flashing and moving content while the child draws follows WCAG 2.2
success criteria 2.3.1 Three Flashes or Below Threshold and 2.2.2 Pause,
Stop, Hide~\cite{wcag22}.}
  \reqfit{Inspect every child-facing screen in each lesson stage and in the
creative space. Pass when each screen offers at most
\texttt{CHILD\_CONTROLS\_MAX} controls and no element animates or
flashes while a stroke is being drawn.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{1}; \srcref{005.05}; \srcref{009.05}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}; \ucref{006}; NFR-UX-002; SAF-MOU-003;
H-MOU-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-002}{Images and icons instead of text}
  \reqshall{Every control on a child-facing screen shall be identified by
an image or icon, and no task on a child-facing screen shall require the
child to read.}
  \reqrationale{Many learners cannot read yet; children navigate games
through images and icons rather than text (\srcref{005.05}, FACT-007).}
  \reqfit{Inspect every child-facing screen. Pass when every control shows
an image or icon, and each child task in \ucref{003} and \ucref{006} can be
completed with all on-screen text hidden.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{1}; FACT-007; \srcref{005.05}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}; \ucref{006}; NFR-UX-001; H-MOU-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-003}{Adult views distinct from child screens}
  \reqshall{Adult views shall look clearly different from child-facing
screens, so that an adult can tell at a glance which kind of screen is
shown.}
  \reqrationale{Specialists described a separate adult interface for
personalizing activities and viewing progress (\srcref{005.02}). A
distinct look helps adults notice when a child has reached an adult-only
screen, alongside the adult check (SAF-RAY-001).}
  \reqfit{Show at least \texttt{UX\_REVIEWERS} adult reviewers a shuffled
set of screenshots of every child-facing screen and adult view, without
titles. Pass when every reviewer sorts every screenshot correctly.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{3}, \goalref{6}; \srcref{005.02}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{001}; \ucref{002}; \ucref{009}; \ucref{010};
SAF-RAY-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Style Requirements}
\label{sec:style}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-004}{Feedback that never marks the drawing
as wrong}
  \reqshall{Feedback on an attempt, including a missed part or a
non-passing attempt, shall use encouraging styling, shall not use error
styling such as warning colours, crosses, alarm sounds or ``wrong''
symbols, and shall not show a missed part by colour alone.}
  \reqrationale{Children become frustrated when an activity keeps
highlighting their mistakes or suggests that their drawing is wrong, so
feedback should recognize effort instead (\srcref{009.02}). Feedback
wording and colours should be positive (\srcref{005.06}). Feedback
describes the drawing, not the child
(BR-006, \mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-022}}). Showing a missed part by more than
colour follows WCAG 2.2 success criterion 1.4.1 Use of
Color~\cite{wcag22}, so a child who does not see the colour still sees
the missed part.}
  \reqfit{For each predefined difficulty, and for a passing and a
non-passing attempt, show a prepared drawing and inspect the feedback.
Pass when every feedback includes an encouraging cue from the approved
feedback list (\mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-014}}), none uses an element on the
error-styling list in this requirement, and every missed part is marked by
an outline, pattern or icon as well as by colour.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{\goalref{1}; \srcref{005.06}; \srcref{009.02}}
  \reqtraces{\mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-014}}; \mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-022}};
\ucref{003}; H-AHM-005; H-MOU-002; H-MOU-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-005}{Consistent look across child screens}
  \reqshall{The same action shall use the same icon, colour and screen
position on every child-facing screen.}
  \reqrationale{Children stay motivated when activities are predictable
(\srcref{009.02}), and a child who navigates by images relies on each icon
meaning the same thing everywhere (\srcref{005.05}). This follows WCAG 2.2
success criteria 3.2.3 Consistent Navigation and 3.2.4 Consistent
Identification~\cite{wcag22}.}
  \reqfit{List every action available on child-facing screens and inspect
each screen where it appears. Pass when each action has one icon, one
colour and one position across all screens.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\srcref{005.05}; \srcref{009.02}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}; \ucref{006}; NFR-UX-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-006}{Appealing but calm style}
  \reqshall{Child-facing screens shall have a style that adult reviewers
judge both appealing to children and calm.}
  \reqrationale{Specialists warned that the design may not be appealing
enough for children, and that the right balance is a playful design in a
calm, minimally stimulating environment (\srcref{009.05}).}
  \reqfit{Show at least \texttt{UX\_REVIEWERS} adult reviewers every
child-facing screen and ask them to rate how appealing to children and
how calm each is, on a five-point scale. Pass when the median of each
rating is at least \texttt{STYLE\_RATING\_MIN}.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{\srcref{009.05}}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-001; NFR-UX-007; H-MOU-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-007}{Style reviewed with educators or
therapists}
  \reqshall{The colours, images and feedback style of child-facing screens
shall be reviewed by at least \texttt{UX\_REVIEWERS} adults, including at
least one educator or occupational therapist, and each change they
request shall be made or recorded with a reason.}
  \reqrationale{Specialists advised designing feedback wording and colours
with educators and occupational therapists, built up bit by bit
(\srcref{005.06}), and involving them throughout design (\srcref{009.06}).
None has been consulted yet (FACT-012).}
  \reqfit{Pass when the review record lists the reviewers and their roles,
includes at least one educator or occupational therapist, and states for
every requested change either that it was made or the reason it was
not.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{\srcref{005.06}; \srcref{009.06}; FACT-012}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-004; NFR-UX-006; H-MOU-001; H-MOU-002}
\end{srsrequirement}
\endgroup

\section{Usability and Humanity Requirements}
\label{sec:usability}
% Long constant names cannot be hyphenated; let lines stretch rather than
% overflow the margin in this section.
\begingroup
\sloppy

This section states how easy Squigglr is to use, personalize and learn,
how its wording treats children and adults, and which accessibility
criteria it meets. As in Section~\ref{sec:look-feel}, fit criteria are
checked by the team or by adult reviewers, and an adult review does not
show that children can use the product (\srcref{005.11}). The adult-set
values for each child are specified in \mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-017}} and
SAF-RAY-008 and are not repeated here.

\subsection{Ease of Use Requirements}
\label{sec:ease-of-use}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-008}{Practice without adult help}
  \reqshall{Once an adult has started a lesson, Squigglr shall need no
adult action through every stage and retry until the lesson ends, other
than a response to a suggestion to go back a stage.}
  \reqrationale{Children should be able to practise without constant
adult help, and parents quickly become annoyed if they must keep
intervening (\srcref{005.05}). Within a lesson the child does not
navigate; Squigglr moves between stages (CA-001,
\mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-023}}).}
  \reqfit{Walk through every catalogue lesson with prepared passing and
non-passing attempts and count the adult actions between the start and
the end of the lesson. Pass when the only adult actions are responses to
suggestions.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{\goalref{1}, \goalref{3}; \srcref{005.05}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{003}; \ucref{005}; \mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-015}};
\mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-023}}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-009}{Quick setup by a new adult}
  \reqshall{An adult using Squigglr for the first time shall be able to
create a child's profile and start a lesson for that child within
\texttt{SETUP\_TIME\_MAX} minutes, with no help from outside Squigglr.}
  \reqrationale{Adults handle setup (\srcref{005.05}), and they are
everyday web users rather than trained staff
(Section~\ref{sec:stakeholders}). Setup that needs help delays
practice.}
  \reqfit{Give at least \texttt{UX\_REVIEWERS} adults who have not used
Squigglr the task ``add a child and start a lesson for them''. Pass when
at least \texttt{UX\_PASS\_RATE} of them finish within
\texttt{SETUP\_TIME\_MAX} minutes without help.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{3}; \srcref{005.05}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{001}; \ucref{002}; NFR-UX-014}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Personalization and Internationalization Requirements}
\label{sec:personalization}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-010}{Audio setting for each child}
  \reqshall{The supporting adult shall be able to turn audio feedback on
or off for each child, and the choice shall apply to every child-facing
screen for that child.}
  \reqrationale{Audio feedback is useful, but users must be able to turn it
off (\srcref{005.07}), and an adult can turn it off if sound upsets a
child (ASM-015). The setting is per child because children's needs differ
(FACT-003).}
  \reqfit{Turn audio off for one prepared profile and on for another. Run a
lesson and the creative space for each. Pass when no sound plays for the
first profile and audio feedback plays for the second.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{FACT-003; ASM-015; \srcref{005.07}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{010}; NFR-UX-021; NFR-UX-022; H-MOU-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-011}{Refinement setting for each child}
  \reqshall{The supporting adult shall be able to turn creative space
refinement on or off for each child; while it is off, the child's strokes
shall be kept exactly as drawn.}
  \reqrationale{Both questionnaire respondents suggested that refinement
be optional or adjustable, and unclear lines can matter to a child's
imagination (\srcref{009.01}). The child can also turn refinement off in
the creative space (\ucref{007}).}
  \reqfit{Turn refinement off for a prepared profile and draw prepared
drawings in the creative space. Pass when every saved stroke matches the
input exactly; turn it on and verify the drawings are refined.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{4}; \srcref{009.01}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; \ucref{010}; H-MOU-006}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-012}{Language-free child screens, one adult
language}
  \reqshall{Child-facing screens shall need no text to be used, and all
text in adult views shall be in \texttt{INTERFACE\_LANGUAGE}.}
  \reqrationale{Children do not need to read to use the child screens,
