while adult tasks such as reviewing records need text (ASM-007). Keeping
language out of the child screens means another adult language would not
change them (NFR-UX-002).}
  \reqfit{Inspect every child-facing screen as in the fit criterion of
NFR-UX-002, and every adult view. Pass when no child task needs text and
all adult-view text is in \texttt{INTERFACE\_LANGUAGE}.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{ASM-007; \srcref{005.05}}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Learning Requirements}
\label{sec:ux-learning}

This subsection covers learning to use Squigglr. Learning to draw is
specified by the FR-LRN requirements.

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-013}{Learning a lesson from its
demonstration}
  \reqshall{The demonstration of each lesson shall show, without words,
where the drawing starts and which way it goes.}
  \reqrationale{Many learners cannot read and learn by watching
(\srcref{005.05}), so the demonstration must show the task without an
adult's explanation. Whether children learn from it can be checked only in
a study with ethics approval, which is out of scope (\srcref{005.11}). The
team's proposed demonstration shows where to start and which way to go
(\intref{Moustafa}{6:03}); the demonstration itself is
\mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-003}}.}
  \reqfit{Show at least \texttt{UX\_REVIEWERS} adults the demonstration of
every catalogue lesson with sound and text hidden. Pass when at least
\texttt{UX\_PASS\_RATE} of them mark the correct start point and direction
for every lesson.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{1}; \srcref{005.05}}
  \reqtraces{\mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-003}}; \ucref{003}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-014}{Adult tasks learnable without
training}
  \reqshall{An adult shall be able to complete each adult task (creating a
profile, choosing a lesson, responding to a suggestion, changing settings
and reviewing progress) without training, using only help shown in
Squigglr.}
  \reqrationale{Adults are not assumed to have training in visual-motor
development or in Squigglr (Section~\ref{sec:stakeholders}), and the
specialists expect adults to handle these tasks (\srcref{005.05}).}
  \reqfit{Give at least \texttt{UX\_REVIEWERS} first-time adults each task.
Pass when at least \texttt{UX\_PASS\_RATE} of them complete every task
without help from outside Squigglr.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{\goalref{3}, \goalref{6}; \srcref{005.05}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{001}; \ucref{002}; \ucref{005}; \ucref{009};
\ucref{010}; NFR-UX-009}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Understandability and Politeness Requirements}
\label{sec:politeness}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-015}{Short, encouraging messages to the
child}
  \reqshall{Every message to the child, including feedback on an attempt
and the end of a lesson, shall be encouraging, shall describe the drawing
rather than the child, and shall be at most \texttt{FEEDBACK\_WORDS\_MAX}
words when spoken.}
  \reqrationale{Feedback wording should be positive (\srcref{005.06}), and
children become frustrated when their drawing is called wrong
(\srcref{009.02}). Feedback describes the drawing, not the child (BR-006,
\mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-022}}). A lesson that ends at its limit must not feel
like a failure (SAF-RAY-005). Messages are short because they are heard
by children who may not read.}
  \reqfit{List every approved feedback message
(\mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-014}}) and the lesson-end message. Pass when each
has at most \texttt{FEEDBACK\_WORDS\_MAX} words, refers to the drawing and
not the child, and uses none of the words ``wrong'', ``bad'', ``fail'' or
``mistake''.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{\goalref{1}; BR-006; \srcref{005.06}; \srcref{009.02}}
  \reqtraces{\mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-014}}; \mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-022}};
NFR-UX-004; \ucref{003}; H-AHM-005; H-MOU-002; H-MOU-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-016}{Effort acknowledged}
  \reqshall{The feedback on every evaluable attempt, including one that
does not pass, shall acknowledge the child's effort before it shows what
to change.}
  \reqrationale{Squigglr should recognize effort and progress, not only
accuracy (\srcref{009.02}).}
  \reqfit{For each predefined difficulty, show a prepared non-passing
attempt. Pass when every feedback message begins with an acknowledgement
from the approved feedback list.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\srcref{009.02}}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-015; \mbox{\reqref{FR-LRN-014}}; \ucref{003};
H-MOU-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-017}{Plain wording in adult views}
  \reqshall{Adult views shall use plain language, shall explain each
glossary term where it first appears in a view, and shall not use
diagnostic, clinical or ranking words.}
  \reqrationale{The TA asked that terms such as ``guidance level'' be
defined rather than left as jargon (\srcref{006}). Records describe
practice only and are not an assessment (BR-006, SAF-RAY-004), and adults
are not assumed to have clinical training
(Section~\ref{sec:stakeholders}).}
  \reqfit{Inspect every adult view against the glossary
(Section~\ref{sec:glossary}) and the words ``diagnosis'', ``disorder'',
``delay'', ``score'', ``rank'' and ``below average''. Pass when every
glossary term used is explained in the view and none of the listed words
appears.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{BR-006; \srcref{006}}
  \reqtraces{SAF-RAY-004; \ucref{009}; \ucref{010}; H-MOU-005}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Accessibility Requirements}
\label{sec:accessibility}

These requirements take specific WCAG 2.2 success
criteria~\cite{wcag22} that the team can check without children, as the
specialists and the supervisor advised (\srcref{005.08}, \srcref{008});
full WCAG conformance is not required. Together with NFR-UX-001,
NFR-UX-004 and NFR-UX-005, they are the criteria Squigglr follows
(OI-08). The stylus is the main input (CON-003); finger input is a
stretch goal and is not required here.

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-018}{Large touch targets}
  \reqshall{Every control on a child-facing screen shall have a touch
target of at least \texttt{TARGET\_SIZE\_MIN}.}
  \reqrationale{Children's grip and fine motor control vary
(\srcref{005.04}), and the supervisor named large icons as a WCAG idea to
adopt (\srcref{008}). The size follows WCAG 2.2 success criterion 2.5.5
Target Size (Enhanced)~\cite{wcag22}.}
  \reqfit{Measure every control on every child-facing screen at the
smallest supported screen size. Pass when every touch target is at least
\texttt{TARGET\_SIZE\_MIN}.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\srcref{005.04}; \srcref{008}}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-002; \ucref{003}; \ucref{006}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-019}{Colour contrast}
  \reqshall{On every screen, text shall have a contrast ratio of at least
4.5:1 with its background, and icons and graphics needed to use a control
at least 3:1.}
  \reqrationale{Some children have sight challenges (\srcref{005.09}). The
ratios follow WCAG 2.2 success criteria 1.4.3 Contrast (Minimum) and
1.4.11 Non-text Contrast~\cite{wcag22}.}
  \reqfit{Check every screen with a contrast checker. Pass when every
piece of text and every control graphic meets its ratio.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\srcref{005.08}; \srcref{005.09}}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-001; NFR-UX-004}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-020}{Text alternatives for icons}
  \reqshall{Every icon or image that acts as a control shall have a text
alternative that assistive technology can read.}
  \reqrationale{An adult who uses a screen reader must still be able to set
up and review practice, and designing for disability serves many groups
(\srcref{005.09}). This follows WCAG 2.2 success criterion 1.1.1 Non-text
Content~\cite{wcag22}. The child does not need the text
(NFR-UX-002).}
  \reqfit{Run an automated accessibility checker on every screen and
inspect each control. Pass when every control has a text alternative that
names its action.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\srcref{005.08}; \srcref{005.09}}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-002; NFR-UX-012}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-021}{Feedback not by sound alone}
  \reqshall{Every message to the child shall be shown on screen and, unless
audio is turned off, also played as sound; no message shall rely on sound
alone.}
  \reqrationale{Offering more than one modality is encouraged in
inclusive design, and the user can turn the audio off (\srcref{005.07}),
so turning it off must not hide any feedback (NFR-UX-010). This follows
WCAG 2.2 success criterion 1.3.3 Sensory Characteristics~\cite{wcag22}.}
  \reqfit{With audio on and then off, show every approved feedback message
and the lesson-end message. Pass when each appears on screen both times,
and sound plays only when audio is on.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\srcref{005.07}}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-010; NFR-UX-015; \ucref{003}; H-MOU-003}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-022}{Control of audio}
  \reqshall{Any sound that plays automatically for more than 3 seconds
shall have a control to pause or stop it.}
  \reqrationale{Too much auditory stimulation can overwhelm children
(\srcref{009.05}). This follows WCAG 2.2 success criterion 1.4.2 Audio
Control~\cite{wcag22}.}
  \reqfit{List every sound Squigglr plays. Pass when each sound longer than
3 seconds can be paused or stopped from the screen it plays on.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\srcref{005.08}; \srcref{009.05}}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-010; H-MOU-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-023}{Keyboard use of adult views}
  \reqshall{Every function in adult views shall be usable with a keyboard
alone.}
  \reqrationale{Adults may review records on a computer
(Section~\ref{sec:stakeholders}), and some cannot use a pointer. This
follows WCAG 2.2 success criterion 2.1.1 Keyboard~\cite{wcag22}. Drawing
on child-facing screens is excluded because it depends on the stylus path
(CON-003).}
  \reqfit{Complete every adult task in \ucref{001}, \ucref{002},
\ucref{005}, \ucref{009} and \ucref{010} using only a keyboard. Pass when
every task can be completed.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{\srcref{005.08}; \srcref{005.09}}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-014}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-UX-024}{Resizable text in adult views}
  \reqshall{Text in adult views shall be resizable to 200 percent without
loss of content or function.}
  \reqrationale{Some adults have sight challenges (\srcref{005.09}). This
follows WCAG 2.2 success criterion 1.4.4 Resize Text~\cite{wcag22}.}
  \reqfit{Zoom the browser to 200 percent on every adult view. Pass when
all text is visible and every function still works.}
  \reqpriority{Could}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{\srcref{005.08}; \srcref{005.09}}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-019}
\end{srsrequirement}
\endgroup

\section{Performance Requirements}
\label{sec:performance}
\subsection{Speed and Latency Requirements}
\label{sec:speed}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-PERF-001}{Stroke feedback latency}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall display visual acknowledgement of a inputted
stroke within \texttt{FEEDBACK\_LATENCY\_MAX} milliseconds of the
stroke's final input point being received.}
  \reqrationale{Delay between stroke input and visual stroke generation
create a poor experience in general and confuse children with learning
difficulties in particular.}
  \reqfit{Pass when the time passed from the final input point to the 
first visible stroke render in each lesson stage is within \texttt{FEEDBACK\_LATENCY\_MAX}.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{1}, \goalref{2}; \srcref{005.05};}
  \reqtraces{\reqref{FR-LRN-007}; \ucref{003}; \ucref{006}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-PERF-002}{AI refinement response latency}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall display the AI refined result within 
\texttt{REFINE\_LATENCY\_MAX} seconds of the final input point being
received on a drawing that includes AI refinement and the canvas remains
open for interaction during that time.}
  \reqrationale{Long, unresponsive pauses break engagement and may be 
interpreted as an error (G.4).}
  \reqfit{Pass when the time passed from the final input point to the 
refined result display is within \texttt{REFINE\_LATENCY\_MAX} seconds and the
canvas accepts new input without any blocking or error during that period.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{4}; \srcref{009.01}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{007}; NFR-UX-011}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-PERF-003}{Progress summary page load}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall display the summary page within
\texttt{SUMMARY\_LOAD\_MAX} seconds of an adult request.}
  \reqrationale{Adults reviewing records expect performance similar 
to everyday web tools (G.6);}
  \reqfit{Passes when time from request to fully visible
summary is within \texttt{SUMMARY\_LOAD\_MAX} seconds for ten
consecutive attempts.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{5}, \goalref{6}; \srcref{005.02}}
  \reqtraces{\ucref{009}; \ucref{010}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Safety-Critical Requirements}
This subsection holds safety requirements that come from the Hazard Analysis
and do not fit another section. Each lists the hazards it mitigates.

\begin{description}
\item[SAF-RAY-001] Squigglr shall allow lesson choice, starting stage
choice, learner switching and acceptance of a go-back suggestion only after
an adult check that a child cannot complete by tapping icons, and shall keep
a pending suggestion, without blocking practice, until an adult accepts or
declines it.\\
\emph{Rationale:} children should be able to practise without constant
adult help (SRC-005, \intref{P1}{34:13}), but progression belongs to adults
(CON-007; SRC-005, \intref{P1}{28:37}). The form of the check is
\texttt{ADULT\_CHECK}, TBD.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} in prepared walkthroughs, no adult-only action
completes without passing the adult check, and a pending suggestion changes
nothing until an adult acts.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-RAY-001, H-RAY-006.
\item[SAF-RAY-002] Before a lesson starts, Squigglr shall check the device
and browser against \texttt{SUPPORTED\_DEVICES} (TBD) and, if they do not
match, show an adult-readable notice that results may be unreliable.\\
\emph{Rationale:} the team does not control the device (CON-001--CON-003).\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} on each prepared unsupported combination the notice
appears before the first attempt; on supported ones it does not.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-RAY-002.
\item[SAF-RAY-003] Squigglr shall keep each completed attempt on the device
until the server confirms it is saved, and shall tell the adult which
attempts could not be saved.\\
\emph{Rationale:} offline access is not promised (SRC-005,
\intref{Moustafa}{6:03}), and G.5 needs complete records.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} with the connection cut during or after prepared
attempts, no confirmed attempt is lost and every unsaved attempt is reported
to the adult.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-RAY-002.
\item[SAF-RAY-004] No record, summary or export shall show a
diagnostic label, a judgement of the child's abilities or a comparison with
other children, and every summary and
export shall state that it describes practice only and is not an assessment
or diagnosis.\\
\emph{Rationale:} Squigglr makes no diagnosis (SRC-001; SRC-005,
\intref{Moustafa}{6:03}), and adults reading records may have no training.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} inspection of every summary and export template finds
none of the excluded content and finds the statement present.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-RAY-003.
\item[SAF-RAY-005] Squigglr shall end a lesson at \texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT},
or at \texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT\_MAX} when extended, without any action from
the child, and shall keep the progress saved up to that point.\\
\emph{Rationale:} the child is not expected to exit a lesson, and no adult
may be watching (CA-001), so repeated failure must end on its own (G.3).\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} in prepared sequences of failed attempts, each lesson
ends at the limit in use with no child input, and the saved record matches
the attempts made.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-RAY-004.
\item[SAF-RAY-006] Squigglr shall record each lesson that ends at
\texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT} or \texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT\_MAX}, and each
improvement extension, show the adult how many times these happened for
each lesson, and keep earlier attempts when a lesson is restarted.\\
\emph{Rationale:} a restart could otherwise hide repeated failure from the
adult.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} in prepared sequences that reach the limit and then
restart, the count and all earlier attempts appear in the adult view.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-RAY-005.
\item[SAF-RAY-007] Squigglr shall identify the active learner on every
practice screen in a form a child who cannot read can recognize, and shall
save every attempt and suggestion to that learner only.\\
\emph{Rationale:} school devices may be shared by several children
(SRC-004).\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} in prepared multi-learner sessions on one device,
every attempt and suggestion is saved under the learner shown when it was
made.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-RAY-006.
\item[SAF-RAY-008] Squigglr shall save a child's adult-set values
(Table~\ref{tab:goalconst}) only within their team-set ranges, with
\texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT} larger than \texttt{DIFFICULTY\_STREAK} and
\texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT\_MAX} at least \texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT}; shall use the middle of each range when the
adult does not choose; shall allow changes only after the adult check; and
shall show the values in use alongside the child's records.\\
\emph{Rationale:} the supervisor advised that the adult chooses these
values (SRC-008); a wrong value can stop a child passing a stage they can
draw, and a child must not change it.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} in prepared walkthroughs, every out-of-range value and
every \texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT} not larger than \texttt{DIFFICULTY\_STREAK}
and every \texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT\_MAX} below \texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT} is
refused, an unset value equals the midpoint, and no change completes
without the adult check.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-RAY-007.

\item[SAF-AHM-001] Squigglr shall prevent duplicate reports of the same
stylus input event from being counted more than once during evaluation or
recording of an attempt, while preserving legitimate separately drawn
strokes, including overlapping strokes.\\
\emph{Rationale:} duplicate input events can inflate drawing coverage,
produce incorrect pass or failure decisions, and cause saved records to
differ from what the learner actually drew (H-AHM-002; BR-004).\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} replay a known one-stroke or multi-stroke input
sequence with and without duplicated input events; verify that the
recorded points, classification and pass result are identical. Replay
two separate deliberately overlapping strokes and verify that both
are preserved.\\
\emph{Related SRS requirements:} FR-LRN-007, FR-LRN-008.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-AHM-002.

\item[SAF-AHM-002] Squigglr shall preserve the relative positions and order
of stylus points within the active drawing surface when the canvas size
or supported device orientation changes, and shall reject input whose
coordinates cannot be mapped reliably to that surface.\\
\emph{Rationale:} an incorrect coordinate mapping can distort a learner's
drawing, produce an inaccurate passing decision, and corrupt stored
attempts (H-AHM-003; BR-004).\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} replay the same prepared stroke sequences before and
after resizing the drawing surface or changing supported device
orientation; verify equivalent relative point positions, stroke order
and pass classifications. Supply invalid or unmappable coordinate values
and verify that none is classified as passing or counted as an evaluable
attempt.\\
\emph{Related SRS requirements:} FR-LRN-007, FR-LRN-010.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-AHM-003.

\item[SAF-SHA-001] Squigglr shall authenticate data at every endpoint where 
it is accessed, encrypt user data with one-way hash, and end the session 
and logout after extended user inactivity. (\texttt{IDLE\_TIMEOUT}, TBD).\\
\emph{Rationale:} personal data, especially that of children's, is confidential. 
unauthorized access to a user account or isolated user information violates
privacy and undermines user trust. Squigglr is a website with no installation
(CON-001, CON-002) so access must be authenticated at server endpoints.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} with mock accounts, while logged in to one account,
attempts to access a wide range of datatypes from another account using 
server commands are rejected; mock account is logged out after \texttt{IDLE\_TIMEOUT}
and attempts to access that accounts information are rejected. \\
\emph{Hazards:} H-SHA-001.

\item[SAF-SHA-002] Squigglr shall confirm that all essential third-party services
do not transmit PII through their public interface.\\
\emph{Rationale:} refinement services may receive raw private data; user data
is protected by privacy commitments; third-party services are implemented as
web dependencies (CON-001) which may transmit user data incongruously.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} confirm that network traffic for each third-party 
contains no PII data transmitted to a non-approved destination.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-SHA-002.

\item[SAF-SHA-003] Squigglr shall inform adult users if a save failed after
with a verification ping after each save.\\
\emph{Rationale:} G.5 requires complete, meaningful activity records;
Adults want to know which sessions contributed to their child's progress
report in detail.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} for 99\% of \texttt{FAILED\_SAVES} (TBD) 
failed save requests, Squigglr does not report the attempt as saved and 
notifies the adult. For 99\% of \texttt{FAILED\_SAVES} successful saves, 
Squigglr does not record a failed save or notify the adult.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-SHA-003.

\item[SAF-SHA-004] Squigglr shall default to pre-prepared fallback content
when the AI refinement tool is unavailable or returning errors or misformatted
content, after which it will send a diagnostic alert to the server and inform 
the adult of the error.\\
\emph{Rationale:} An unhandled provider error interrupting a child's drawing
session would violate G.4, and failing to catch the error creates confusion
for the child with unpredictable outputs.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} fallback content replaces the provided content when
purposefully incorrectly formatted content is submitted and when a mock 
server fails to provide a response; The adult user is informed in both cases.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-SHA-004.

\item[SAF-SHA-005] Squigglr shall allow users to report inappropriate AI
generated content through a form; human reviewers shall use catalogued
confirmed incorrect output to potentially update API guardrails and 
permissiveness.\\
\emph{Rationale:} Raw provider output may be inaccurate or inappropriate;
Content moderation must adapt to new, unforseen inputs as the application
grows in userbase.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} deliberate attempts to recreate inappropriate output 
by imitating report diagnostics of a manufactured inappropriate output case 
do not yield similarly inappropriate results after content moderation update.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-SHA-005

\item[SAF-MOU-001] In guided lessons, feedback on a missed part shall show
the child where the part is missing without drawing it for them.\\
\emph{Rationale:} the aim of practice is drawing with gradually less help
(G.2); if Squigglr completes the drawing, the child can come to rely on it
(H-MOU-004). The child's actual strokes are what is recorded (BR-004).\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} for each predefined difficulty, show a prepared
attempt with a missed part and compare the strokes on screen and in the
record before and after the feedback. Pass when the feedback adds no
stroke to the attempt.\\
\emph{Related SRS requirements:} FR-LRN-014, FR-LRN-023, BR-004.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-MOU-004.

\item[SAF-MOU-002] Records and progress summaries shall keep attempts
made with a guide separate from attempts made without one,
shall not include creative space drawings, and shall not present the
number of attempts as a measure of the child's skill.\\
\emph{Rationale:} a summary that blurs assisted and independent attempts,
counts refined drawings as practice or treats fewer attempts as better
skill can lead an adult to move the child on too early or hold them back
(H-MOU-005; SRC-005, \intref{Moustafa}{6:03}). Refinement must never reach
the records adults rely on (BR-005).\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} for prepared histories with known assisted,
independent and creative space work, check every summary and record. Pass
when assisted and independent attempts are shown separately, no
creative space drawing appears, and no view ranks or rates the child by
the number of attempts.\\
\emph{Related SRS requirements:} G.6, FR-LRN-012, SAF-RAY-004,
NFR-UX-017.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-MOU-005.

\item[SAF-MOU-003] No content on any child-facing screen shall flash more
than three times in any one-second period.\\
\emph{Rationale:} flashing can trigger seizures in rare cases, the only
physical harm in the hazard analysis (H-MOU-001). NFR-UX-001 bans flashing
while the child is drawing, but feedback, the lesson-end message and screen
transitions also appear on child-facing screens. This follows WCAG 2.2
success criterion 2.3.1 Three Flashes or Below Threshold~\cite{wcag22}.\\
\emph{Fit criterion:} record every child-facing screen, including all
feedback, the lesson-end message and every transition, and count the
flashes in each one-second window. Pass when no window has more than three
flashes.\\
\emph{Related SRS requirements:} NFR-UX-001.\\
\emph{Hazards:} H-MOU-001.

\end{description}
\subsection{Precision or Accuracy Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-PERF-004}{Stage result scoring accuracy}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall compute a deterministic stage result 
score for all attempts: the same strokes and stage criteria shall
produce the same evaluation.}
  \reqrationale{Adults use stage results to monitor a child's progress
(G.5, G.6). Non-deterministic may create arbitrary or difficult to
interpret scoring not suitable for progress tracking. Deterministic
scoring is also easier to implement and non-deterministic scoring has
no clear benefits.}
  \reqfit{Pass when identical programmed strokes (done with an app like
like selenium) on the same stage ten times produce identical scores}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{2}; \goalref{5}; \goalref{6}}
  \reqtraces{\reqref{FR-LRN-007}; \reqref{FR-LRN-008}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-PERF-005}{Creative refinement proximity}
  \reqshall{Squiqqlr shall keep every point of a refined stroke
within \texttt{REFINE\_TOLERANCE} of the child's corresponding
original stroke.}
  \reqrationale{Refinement is intended to keep the child's drawing
recognisably as their own work (G.4, BR-005). Deviation too far from 
the original would mean replacement rather than refinement.}
  \reqfit{Pass when every item in the supported creative space set
does not exceed the distance scale of \texttt{REFINE\_TOLERANCE} 
compared to the original item.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{4};}
  \reqtraces{\reqref{NFR-PERF-005}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Robustness or Fault-Tolerance Requirements}
\label{sec:robustness}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-PERF-006}{Attempt preservation on network loss}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall retain any completed attempt on the device if 
connection is lost during the attempt or after the latest attempt until 
the server confirms it is saved (after connection is restored).}
  \reqrationale{G.5 requires complete practice records; losing their current 
attempt may upset a child.
\reqref{SAF-RAY-003}.}
  \reqfit{Pass when attempts are saved after connection is intentionally
cut during a mock attempt three times consecutively.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{5}; \srcref{005.02}}
  \reqtraces{\reqref{SAF-RAY-003}; \ucref{003}; \ucref{006}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-PERF-007}{Refinement provider fallback}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall display the child's original unrefined 
strokes when the AI refinement service is unavailable or returns 
misformatted or inappropriate output.}
  \reqrationale{Ignoring AI refinement in the rare cases where
the output is missing or unuseable creates a smoother experience
for the child and prioritizes avoiding interruptions to acquiring
full refinement coverage. Complements \reqref{SAF-SHA-004}.}
  \reqfit{Pass when the childs original strokes are kept ten times
consecutively after intentionally returning misformatted outputs or 
making the mock service unavailable.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{4}; \srcref{009.01}}
  \reqtraces{\reqref{SAF-SHA-004}; \ucref{003}; \ucref{006}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-PERF-008}{Save failure transparency}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall not report a successful save if a 
save operation fails and shall instead display a visible notification 
to the adult describing which record was not saved.}
  \reqrationale{G.5 requires complete and meaningful practice records.
Silently losing a save and reporting success would inform the adult 
based on a record that does not reflect the child's actual practice. 
Complements \reqref{SAF-SHA-003}.}
  \reqfit{Pass when Squigglr does not display a success confirmation and 
instead shows a clearly visible failure notification to the adult identifying
the unsaved record.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{5}; \srcref{005.02}}
  \reqtraces{\reqref{SAF-SHA-003}; \ucref{009}; \ucref{010}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Capacity Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-PERF-009}{Concurrent session capacity}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall support a concurrent session target of 
\texttt{CONCURRENT\_CAPACITY} (TBD) simultaneous active users without 
degrading response times below the thresholds stated in 
Section~\ref{sec:speed} (NFR-PERF-001, NFR-PERF-002, and NFR-PERF-003).}
  \reqrationale{The anticipated system deployment is to a small number 
of schools or households. The exact session target is deferred to
the VnV Plan.}
  \reqfit{To be defined in the VnV Plan; pass when the system sustains
the latency thresholds outlined in Section~\ref{sec:speed} under the 
agreed concurrent load without error or failure.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{\goalref{1}; \goalref{5}}
  \reqtraces{\reqref{NFR-PERF-001}; \reqref{NFR-PERF-002}; \reqref{NFR-PERF-003}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Scalability or Extensibility Requirements}

Adding or removing lessons from the Squigglr lesson catalogue requires
no change to the lesson execution logic or any other module's source
code. Lesson content is stored as data and read at runtime
(LC-001, Section~\ref{sec:changes}). This is a design-level decision; no
standalone NFR is required here because the constraint is already
captured by \reqref{NFR-MAINT-001}.

\subsection{Longevity Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-PERF-010}{Operational lifetime through capstone}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall remain fully functional on the supported
browsers and devices (OI-09) through PH-3, the end of the capstone project period.}
  \reqrationale{After final evaluation and the exposition, post capstone 
maintenance is outside the project scope.}
  \reqfit{Pass when Squigglr can be launched and all features exercised
on the supported browsers and devices as listed in OI-09 at the PH-3
milestone; any browser or device that fails this check shall be
removed from the supported list and OI-09 updated accordingly.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{\goalref{1}; \goalref{2}; \goalref{3}}
  \reqtraces{\reqref{NFR-OPS-002}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\section{Operational and Environmental Requirements}
\subsection{Expected Physical Environment}

Squigglr is meant for use on a touchscreen tablet or laptop (CON-003).
A stylus is required for interaction with motor practice features. Finger 
input is a stretch goal (LC-006). Expected settings include the domicile,
classroom, and therapy centres where children draw (FACT-009) often with 
an adult nearby. The device is expected to be placed on a flat surface or 
held in the user's hands.

\subsection{Wider Environment Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-OPS-001}{HTTPS-only communication}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall exchange all data between the browser client
and server under encrypted HTTPS protocol.}
  \reqrationale{Account data travels over networks that the Squigglr team 
does not control (ASM-005). HTTPS prevents unauthorized access and tampering 
in transit. School and other facility networks may also block non-HTTPS traffic.}
  \reqfit{Confirm that every request made by Squigglr uses the \texttt{https://} 
scheme. Verify that navigating to the \texttt{http://} URL either redirects to 
HTTPS or is blocked.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{\goalref{5}; \goalref{6};}
  \reqtraces{ASM-005; ASM-013; CON-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Requirements for Interfacing with Adjacent Systems}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-OPS-002}{Adjacent system interfaces}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall interact with the following external systems:
\begin{description}
  \item[Authentication and session provider.] Squigglr shall delegate
  account creation, login, and session management to a third-party
  authentication provider.
  \item[AI refinement service.] Squigglr shall send the child's raw stroke
  data (without child PII \reqref{SAF-SHA-002}) to a third-party AI model 
  for visual refinement and shall receive refined stroke data in return.
  \item[Hosting and content-delivery network (Supabase / Vercel).] Squigglr
  shall be served from Vercel for the front-end and Supabase for back-end for
  application assets.
  For authentication and hosting, Squigglr shall display an error message 
  in the event of provider unavailability until the availability returns.
\end{description}}
  \reqrationale{The choice of Supabase and Vercel is arbitrary and abstract
descriptions of adjacent systems leaves the requirements open to substitutes.}
  \reqfit{Confirm that the error message displays for a mock outage of the 
  authentication and hosting services. (For AI refinement, provider 
  unavailability protocol is described in \reqref{NFR-PERF-007})}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{5}; \goalref{6}; \srcref{003}; \srcref{004}}
  \reqtraces{ASM-005; ASM-013; \reqref{NFR-PERF-007}; \ucref{001}; \ucref{005}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Productization Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-OPS-003}{No installation required}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall require no download, plugin, or
browser extension and will be usable directly from a supported
browser.}
  \reqrationale{Installing applications on centrally administered device
ecosystems is difficult or impossible without administrator approval
(CON-001, CON-002) while a website URL is much easier to access.}
  \reqfit{Passes when lesson and account features on Squigglr can
be opened on each supported browser and device combination (OI-09)
without installation}.
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{CON-001; CON-002; \srcref{003}; \srcref{004}}
  \reqtraces{CON-001; CON-002; OI-09;}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Release Requirements}

Squigglr has no installation package. A release is a deployment to the
hosting platform (Vercel) triggered by merging to the production branch.

\section{Maintainability and Support Requirements}
\subsection{Maintenance Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-MAINT-001}{Lesson catalogue update without code change}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall require no change to the source code to add, remove,
or editing a lesson from the lesson catalogue; all lessons shall be stored as data
read at runtime.}
  \reqrationale{Lessons will need to be updated and the catalogue will need to be
added to as the userbase grows and the development team learns from previous 
experience. An applications with an extensible content system should use a 
data-driven content system for easier uploading and eventually offloading of 
content creation to non-code teams.}
  \reqfit{Confirm that lessons are stored as data objects and that the application
renders them at runtime from these objects. Successfully upload, delete, and edit
a lesson in a developer view.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{}
  \reqtraces{\reqref{FR-LRN-001}; \reqref{FR-LRN-002}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-MAINT-002}{Programmer locatability of stage behaviour}
  \reqshall{A team member unfamiliar with the codebase shall be able
to locate the module responsible for a given stage behaviour within
\texttt{LOCATE\_TIME\_MAX} minutes.}
  \reqrationale{Future contributions must be easy to implement as the application
will need many iterations with a growing user base.}
  \reqfit{Passes if a team member unfamiliar with the codebase reads a description
of a stage behaviour written by a team member familiar with the codebase and takes
less than \texttt{LOCATE\_TIME\_MAX} minutes to find the corresponding module.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\srcref{008}}
  \reqtraces{\reqref{FR-LRN-007}; \reqref{FR-LRN-008}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Supportability Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-MAINT-003}{Module interface descriptions consistent with MG/MIS}
  \reqshall{Each top-level Squigglr module shall have an interface
description (exported functions, types, and pre/post-conditions).}
  \reqrationale{Defining the contract between modules via the service
they provide and those using it improves comprehensibility as different
teams work on multiple modules asynchronously.}
  \reqfit{Confirm that every issue created for development of a module
has a requirement for an interface description.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{}
  \reqtraces{NFR-MAINT-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-MAINT-004}{Consistent coding style}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall be developed along with a CI pipeline 
that rejects any commit that introduces a violation of pre-configured
linter or formatter rules determined by the team.}
  \reqrationale{A consistent style is standard practice and reduces
confusion among contributors.}
  \reqfit{Confirm that the CI pipeline rejects commits that introduces 
a linter or formatter violation by testing two different violations.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{}
  \reqtraces{NFR-MAINT-002}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Adaptability Requirements}

The adaptability of Squigglr is addressed at the design level through
the likely changes catalogue (LC-001 through LC-008) in Section~\ref{sec:changes}. 
Some likely changes are already captured by requirements like LC-001 (stored lesson 
changes) in \reqref{NFR-MAINT-001} and LC-004 in \reqref{NFR-OPS-002} No standalone 
NFR is required here. 

\section{Security Requirements}
\subsection{Access Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-SEC-001}{Adult account data access}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall only allow the adult owner of an account
to read and write to their respective child's profile.}
  \reqrationale{Each adult account holds one or more child learner profiles
(BR-001). Allowing unauthorized users to modify or read other childrens accounts
violates privacy and undermines trust in the application.}
  \reqfit{Using two test accounts, while authenticated as account A attempt
to read or modify a child profile and attempt records belonging to account B
via direct server requests. Pass when every such request is rejected and
account A's own data is unchanged.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{5}; \goalref{6}; \srcref{004}}
  \reqtraces{SAF-SHA-001; BR-001; \ucref{009}; \ucref{010}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-SEC-002}{Session token invalidation on deliberate logout}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall invalidate the corresponding session token of an adult
user when they deliberately log out so that a captured or replayed copy of that token 
cannot be used to access the account afterwards.}
  \reqrationale{A token captured during an active session (e.g.\ from a shared-device
browser history or network log) could otherwise remain valid indefinitely (OI-01). Explicit
logout is the adult's signal that the device may pass to another user.}
  \reqfit{Obtain a valid session token while authenticated, log out deliberately, 
and attempt to make an authenticated API request by reusing the token. 
Pass when the request is rejected three times consecutively.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{5}; \goalref{6}}
  \reqtraces{SAF-SHA-001; NFR-SEC-001; OI-01}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Integrity Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-SEC-003}{Immutability of confirmed attempt records}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall not allow an attempt record that has been confirmed 
saved by the server to be modifiable by any subsequent client request.}
  \reqrationale{Immutability on attempt records ensures adult trust in the
accuracy of their child's records (G.5). The option to delete account information
on request is standard, but changing existing records threatens non-repudiation.}
  \reqfit{Passes when a modification request on an already confirmed saved record
fails consecutively three times with an appropriate error message logged.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{\goalref{5}; \goalref{6}}
  \reqtraces{SAF-AHM-001; SAF-AHM-002; SAF-SHA-003; FR-LRN-013}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Privacy Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-SEC-004}{Minimal personal data collection}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall not store any child-identifying information
beyond a non-identifying username.}
  \reqrationale{Children's PII are not necessary for Squigglr to function
and minimizing collected data limits security risks. The supervising
adult's contact details are collected at account registration if any
identifying information is needed anyways; This satisfies Squigglr's 
accountability, consent, and access obligations under PIPEDA~\cite{pipeda}.}
  \reqfit{Passes if there is no field during child registration beyond
a username that does not prompt for their real name.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-1}
  \reqsources{\goalref{5}; \goalref{6}; \srcref{004}}
  \reqtraces{BR-001; NFR-SEC-001}
\end{srsrequirement}

\emph{Note:} Institutional self-hosting or per-institution data partitioning 
is out of scope for this project. Account-level access control
is already enforced by \reqref{NFR-SEC-001} and \reqref{SAF-SHA-001},
which is sufficient for a single-instance capstone project like Squigglr.

\subsection{Audit Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-SEC-004}{Server-side logging of authentication events}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall record every failed login session-timeout event 
in a server-side audit log; the log entries shall not
be accessible to adult users through any application interface.}
  \reqrationale{Audit logs are useful for security as they provide diagnostics
after abnormal behavior like brute-force attacks. Logs are hidden from users
because they reveal personal information and users have no legitimate use for 
them in most cases.}
  \reqfit{Trigger ten failed login attempts and ten logouts by inactivity.
Confirm that all events are recorded in the audit log with a time stamp
and that a mock adult account is denied access to the audit log through request.}
  \reqpriority{Should}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{}
  \reqtraces{SAF-SHA-001; NFR-SEC-001; NFR-SEC-007}
\end{srsrequirement}

\subsection{Immunity Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-SEC-005}{Input sanitisation against injection}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall sanitise all text inputs supplied by a user
to prevent injection attacks, including but not limited to SQL injection 
and cross-site scripting (XSS).}
  \reqfit{Passes if a pre-prepared set of SQL injections and XSS probe strings 
  are sanitized through every text submission field in the application and API
  text submission endpoint.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{}
  \reqtraces{SAF-AHM-001; NFR-SEC-001; NFR-SEC-002}
\end{srsrequirement}


\section{Cultural Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-CULT-001}{Interface Language of Adult Views}
  \reqshall{All text in adult views shall be in \texttt{INTERFACE\_LANGUAGE}.}
  \reqrationale{Required by \reqref{NFR-UX-012} and assumes that every adult
who uses Squigglr understands that language (ASM-007). Child-facing screens 
contain no text as all controls are identified by images or icons (\reqref{NFR-UX-002}), 
so child screens carry no cultural localisation.}
  \reqfit{Confirm that every text string in the adult view is in 
\texttt{INTERFACE\_LANGUAGE}.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-2}
  \reqsources{ASM-007}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-012; NFR-UX-002;}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-CULT-002}{Cultural Sensitivity of Symbols and Imagery}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall contain no symbols or imagery that carry a known negative 
cultural meaning in communities likely to use Squigglr.}
  \reqrationale{The style review required by \reqref{NFR-UX-007} should assesses 
for such associations in to aesthetic choices and clarity as such associations
may not be obvious to the development team; negative symbols can distress a child
and create a negative experience for both the child and adult.}
  \reqfit{Passes if style review finds no negative cultural associations or if concerns
raised by the style review are adressed and approved by the reviewers.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{}
  \reqtraces{NFR-UX-007}
\end{srsrequirement}


\section{Compliance Requirements}

\subsection{Legal Requirements}
\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-COMP-001}{PIPEDA}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall comply with applicable obligations under 
PIPEDA~\cite{pipeda}.}
  \reqrationale{PIPEDA applies because Squigglr is a Canadian project 
hosted for Canadian schools that collects personal information in the 
course of commercial-like activity.}
  \reqfit{Confirm that Squigglr's collection and handling of personal 
information complies with PIPEDA obligations.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{}
  \reqtraces{PIPEDA~\cite{pipeda}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-COMP-002}{Ontario FIPPA Applicability}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall be compliant with the Ontario Freedom
of Information and Protection of Privacy Act (FIPPA) before adoption
by any school boards.}
  \reqrationale{Applicability is TBD, adoption by school board is out of the scope of the project.}
  \reqfit{Before any Ontario school board adoption, confirm that Squigglr is compliant with FIPPA.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{}
  \reqtraces{}
\end{srsrequirement}

\emph{Note:} GDPR does not apply because EU jurisdiction is out of 
scope for Squigglr. COPPA does not apply because US jurisdiction 
is out of scope for Squigglr and child-identifying data is not 
collected (BR-001).

\subsection{Standards Compliance Requirements}

\begin{srsrequirement}{NFR-COMP-005}{WCAG 2.2 Success Criteria}
  \reqshall{Squigglr shall meet the selected WCAG 2.2 success 
criteria~\cite{wcag22} encompassed by \reqref{NFR-UX-001}, 
\reqref{NFR-UX-004}, \reqref{NFR-UX-005}, and \reqref{NFR-UX-018}--\reqref{NFR-UX-023}.}
  \reqrationale{The selected criteria follow the specialist 
  recommendation (SRC-005), supervisor advice (SRC-008), and OI-08. 
  Full WCAG conformance is a stretch goal (Section~25).}
  \reqfit{Confirm that Squigglr is compliant with the listed WCAG 2.2 success criteria.}
  \reqpriority{Must}
  \reqphase{PH-3}
  \reqsources{}
  \reqtraces{WCAG 2.2~\cite{wcag22}}
\end{srsrequirement}

\section{Open Issues}
\label{sec:open-issues}

This section lists questions that are not yet settled and could change the
requirements. Each issue has an identifier (OI-\#\#), an owner, the decision
or evidence needed to close it, the goals, constraints or requirements it
affects, and a target date. Requirement identifiers are linked here as the
requirements are written. Target dates come from the SRS schedule (review
copy Oct.\ 17, 2026; submission Oct.\ 19, 2026) or from the course deadlines
in Table~\ref{tab:con-schedule} (CON-004) where an issue cannot be closed
before submission.

>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.11\textwidth}@{}}

OI-01 & \emph{School and shared devices.} School devices may be shared by
several children (SRC-004) and are often managed centrally (CON-002).
One adult account holds several learner profiles, and an adult switches
between them (BR-001). Still needed: whether an adult signs in on each
shared device, and how a device is left when a session ends. &
G.5, G.6, CON-001, CON-002; requirement IDs to be linked when written &
Shamil Canbolat & Oct.\ 17, 2026 \\
\addlinespace
OI-02 & \emph{Privacy of practice records.} The supervisor's advice to
collect no information about children
(SRC-004, \href{https://github.com/AHMEDELZARIA/squigglr/issues/21}{\#21})
concerns personal information; storing practice attempts is acceptable.
Still needed: the privacy requirements for attempt records, including who
can see them, whether and how they can be shared, and how they are
deleted. &
G.5, G.6, CON-005; privacy requirement IDs to be linked when written &
Shamil Canbolat & Oct.\ 17, 2026 \\
\addlinespace
OI-03 & \emph{Scope of G.4.} A peer reviewer suggested moving creative
drawing and refinement to the stretch goals
(\href{https://github.com/AHMEDELZARIA/squigglr/issues/70}{\#70}). The
team may adopt the change or reply on the issue explaining why G.4 stays a
core goal (SRC-006). Still needed: the reply on the issue and, if G.4
stays, a supported set of items small enough for the schedule. &
G.4 & Muhammad Huzaifah & Oct.\ 17, 2026 \\
\addlinespace
OI-04 & \emph{TA confirmation of revised G.3.} The team distinguishes
adult selection of lessons, starting stages, skips and backward moves
from automatic advancement after a passing attempt within the selected
lesson (G.2, BR-002, CON-007). G.3 permits an optional suggestion to go
back a stage after
\texttt{DIFFICULTY\_STREAK} failed attempts and a lesson end after
\texttt{ATTEMPT\_LIMIT}, held back while the child is improving
(\texttt{IMPROVE\_WINDOW}, \texttt{ATTEMPT\_EXTENSION}). The TA agreed to the
adult-control revision in principle and asked that the updated PSG be
merged after marking the submitted version (SRC-006). Still needed:
confirmation of the rule as clarified, not a new choice between automatic
and adult-authorized forward progression. &
G.3, CON-007 & Shamil Canbolat & Oct.\ 17, 2026 \\

Section~\ref{sec:constants} defines the ranges and defaults for the six

Verify the selected ranges with adult-made drawings, provide examples
illustrating the endpoints of the \texttt{STAGE\_PASS} range, and
confirm that the drawing-evaluation method produces results on the
specified 0--100 scale (SRC-004, SRC-008). No child testing is permitted. &

\reqref{FR-LRN-021} &

this project and, if so, the access and privacy requirements that keep
their records apart. &

\section{Off-the-Shelf Solutions}
\label{sec:ots}

This section surveys the products and components that already exist and
records how closely each matches Squigglr's goals. The survey gives the
comparison promised in the User Business subsection and answers two
peer-review issues (SRC-007): what existing handwriting, tracing and
motor-skill apps do and what gap remains
(\href{https://github.com/AHMEDELZARIA/squigglr/issues/69}{\#69}), and
which existing models the team might use for refinement
(\href{https://github.com/AHMEDELZARIA/squigglr/issues/74}{\#74}). As the
Volere template intends, the survey shows that alternatives were considered
and how closely they match the needs; it is not a feasibility study
\citep{robertson2012volere}. Nothing here is a requirement or a
design decision. Every product, library and model named is a candidate the
team may evaluate, and the Development Plan leaves the choice of model open
until the proof-of-concept work (SRC-002).

The findings are preliminary desk research, done on Oct.\ 15, 2026. The
team searched the web, Apple's App Store and Google Play for handwriting
apps, letter and shape tracing apps (including web-based ones), children's
drawing apps, and fine-motor or occupational therapy practice apps, then
read each candidate's own store listing, website or documentation. Every
statement about a product comes from those sources, cited below. No
product was installed or tested, and no child took part (CON-005), so a
missing feature means only that the sources read do not describe it.
Prices are United States prices on that date, given only where the vendor
states them. The specialist interview did not cover existing apps
(FACT-013); the follow-up questionnaire (SRC-009) covered refinement in the
creative space and letter activities.

Table~\ref{tab:ots-dimensions} lists the dimensions used to compare
products. Each comes from a goal, constraint or business rule, so the
comparison measures fit to this project, not general quality. The
Reusable Components subsection adds the Development Plan's criteria for
choosing a refinement approach. The data a product collects about a child
(BR-001) is not a dimension: vendors describe their data handling in
different terms, and Squigglr's own privacy requirements are still open
(OI-02). The privacy terms of candidate refinement services are compared
in the Reusable Components subsection instead.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\small
\caption{Dimensions used to compare existing products with Squigglr}
\label{tab:ots-dimensions}
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}l>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X%
>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.2\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Dim.} & \textbf{What Squigglr needs} & \textbf{Source} \\
\midrule
D1 & Graded guidance with a stylus: each lesson on a line, shape or simple
drawing moves through stages that give less help, from a demonstration to
drawing with no guide. & G.1, G.2, CON-003 \\
D2 & Adult control: the adult chooses the lesson and the starting stage,
and the child goes back a stage only if an adult accepts. & G.3, CON-007,
BR-003 \\
D3 & Records of the process: each attempt keeps the child's actual
strokes, the stage (the guide used), every predefined difficulty, later
corrections and the outcome. & G.5, BR-004 \\
D4 & Adult summaries without judgement: attempts made with a guide are
shown apart from attempts made without one, with no score, diagnostic
label or comparison with other children. & G.6, BR-006 \\
D5 & Runs in a web browser with no installation. & CON-001, CON-002 \\
D6 & Minimal, editable refinement: in a creative space the child's own
strokes are adjusted within \texttt{REFINE\_TOLERANCE}, stay editable,
and the original can be restored. & G.4, BR-005 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{table}

\subsection{Ready-Made Products}
\label{sec:ots-products}
The survey kept twelve products in five groups. Together they cover the
kinds of product that the search found a supporting adult might use
instead of Squigglr, plus the drawing-assistance features closest to
refinement. Each product is
described below from its vendor's sources, and Table~\ref{tab:ots-matrix}
scores it against the dimensions of Table~\ref{tab:ots-dimensions}.

\paragraph{Handwriting and tracing apps.}
Kaligo, by Learn \& Go, is a handwriting and literacy app for tablets that
recommends a stylus, with a web portal for teachers
\citep{LearnAndGo2026Kaligo,LearnAndGo2026Requirements}. Teachers assign
and create lessons, set the difficulty and visual guidance for each
student, view each completed exercise as a replay or screenshot, and
follow class progress. The vendor also offers a screening use in which
artificial intelligence (AI) assesses handwriting and fine motor skills to
identify students who need support \citep{LearnAndGo2026Teachers}, and the
App Store listing describes a learning experience that adapts to each
child's pace \citep{LearnAndGo2026Kaligo}. The app is installed from an
app store, and an internet connection is needed to synchronise lessons and
results \citep{LearnAndGo2026Requirements}. The download is free; school
prices are not stated on the pages read.

Wet-Dry-Try, from Learning Without Tears, is an iPad app based on the
Handwriting Without Tears curriculum. Children practise capitals, numbers
and lowercase letters in step-by-step sequences in a developmental order,
a virtual coach gives spoken feedback on errors, up to three children can
share one device, and the settings offer three sensitivity levels
\citep{LearningWithoutTears2026WetDryTryApp}. A classroom licence costs
US\$3.99 per student per year \citep{LearningWithoutTears2026WetDryTry}.

LetterSchool takes each letter, number or geometric shape through three
games that give less help each time: tap the dots in order, trace the
path, then write it from memory; earning a star unlocks the next game
\citep{LetterSchool2026HowWhy,LetterSchool2026Play}. A ``Golden'' level
displays the child's exact writing so that adults can track progress, and
progress is stored for up to three players on one device. On Android the
first five letters, numbers and shapes are free, and the rest is a
one-time purchase
\citep{LetterSchool2026Play}.

Writing Wizard, by L'Escapadou, traces letters, numbers, words and, for
toddlers, shapes. Adults set the letter size, the difficulty and whether
the model is shown; a five-star mode asks the child to trace an item five
times, each harder than the last; and reports let adults replay and export
what the child traced \citep{Abel2026WritingWizard}. Installed versions
exist for iPad, iPhone, Android, Kindle Fire and Windows
\citep{LEscapadou2026WritingWizard}, and the school edition costs
US\$13.99 on the App Store \citep{Abel2026WritingWizard}.

\paragraph{Fine-motor and occupational therapy practice apps.}
Dexteria, by BinaryLabs, is an iPhone and iPad app for practising fine
motor skills and handwriting readiness. Dexteria creates performance and
usage reports that can be emailed, its companion app compares performance
with reference ranges and produces reports for school documentation, and
the vendor states that it is not a medical device; the app costs US\$6.99
\citep{BinaryLabs2026Dexteria}. Dynamilis, by School Rebound, is an iPad
app for children aged five to twelve. Dynamilis analyses handwriting
written with an Apple Pencil or Logitech Crayon, gives a handwriting
profile and recommends a specialist when the score falls below a
threshold, offers personalised practice activities, and has a replay mode
for its analysis; a subscription costs US\$14.99 a month or US\$89.99 a
year \citep{SchoolRebound2026Dynamilis}.

\paragraph{Web-based tracing tools.}
Tracing Town's Tracing Playground runs in the browser with no account or
download and accepts touch, stylus or mouse. A start dot, an arrow and
numbered strokes guide each stroke of a shape or letter, the page says in
words when the finger leaves the path, nothing is timed or scored, and
nothing is recorded or sent from the device
\citep{TracingTown2026Playground}.

\paragraph{Children's drawing apps.}
The Crayola Create and Play app, which also has an online version, offers
drawing, colouring and educational activities for children aged three to
eight, by subscription after a free trial; the vendor states that it has
no rules, points or time limits \citep{Crayola2026CreateAndPlay}. Tux
Paint is a free, open-source drawing program released under version~2 of
the GNU General Public License (GPL), installed on Windows, macOS, Linux,
Android and other systems \citep{TuxPaint2026Download}.

\paragraph{Drawing-assistance features.}
Google's AutoDraw is a free web tool that guesses what the user is drawing
and suggests drawings made by artists \citep{Motzenbecker2017AutoDraw};
choosing a suggestion replaces the user's sketch
\citep{Lardinois2017AutoDraw}. Smart Script, part of iPadOS~18, smooths
and straightens text handwritten with an Apple Pencil while keeping the
look of the writer's own handwriting \citep{Apple2024SmartScript}.
QuickShape, in the Procreate drawing app, snaps a held stroke into a
perfect line, arc, ellipse, triangle or quadrilateral that can then be
edited through its nodes \citep{Procreate2026QuickShape}.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\footnotesize
\caption{Existing products scored against the comparison dimensions}
\label{tab:ots-matrix}
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.2\textwidth}%
>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}Xcccccc@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Product} & \textbf{Delivery} & \textbf{D1} & \textbf{D2} &
\textbf{D3} & \textbf{D4} & \textbf{D5} & \textbf{D6} \\
\midrule
Kaligo & Tablet app; teacher web portal & Part & Part & Part & No & No
& -- \\
Wet-Dry-Try & iPad app & Part & Part & -- & -- & No & -- \\
LetterSchool & Tablet and phone app & Yes & -- & Part & -- & No & -- \\
Writing Wizard & Installed app & Part & Part & Part & -- & No & -- \\
Dexteria & iPhone and iPad app & -- & -- & Part & No & No & -- \\
Dynamilis & iPad app & -- & -- & Part & No & No & -- \\
Tracing Playground & Web page & Part & -- & No & No & Yes & -- \\
Crayola Create and Play & App and online version & -- & -- & -- & -- &
Part & -- \\
Tux Paint & Installed program & -- & -- & -- & -- & No & -- \\
AutoDraw & Web tool & -- & -- & -- & -- & Yes & No \\

\end{tabularx}

\smallskip
\begin{minipage}{\textwidth}
\raggedright
Yes: the sources describe the feature. Part: they describe a weaker or
different form, explained in the text. No: they describe the opposite,
such as an installed app, a score or no records at all. --: the sources
read do not describe it, which does not show that the product lacks it.
\end{minipage}
\end{table}

The ``Part'' marks in Table~\ref{tab:ots-matrix} have these reasons. For
D1, Kaligo lets teachers adjust visual guidance but describes no fixed
sequence of stages, Wet-Dry-Try's step-by-step sequences cover only
letters and numbers, Writing Wizard's five-star mode is mainly for letters
and words, and Tracing Playground has a single guided level. For D2,
Kaligo, Wet-Dry-Try and Writing Wizard let adults set difficulty,
guidance or sensitivity, but none describes an adult choosing the
starting point within a graded sequence, and Kaligo also adapts on its
own. For D3, the products that keep anything keep a replay, a screenshot
or the final writing (Kaligo, Writing Wizard, Dynamilis, LetterSchool) or
usage reports (Dexteria). For D5, Crayola Create and Play has an online
version beside its app. For D6, Smart Script refines handwritten text, not
drawings, and QuickShape replaces a stroke with a perfect form, which is
more than a minimal change.

\paragraph{Gap.}
No surveyed product combines the six dimensions, and three gaps stand out.
First, records of the process (G.5): none of the sources read describes a
record of which guide the child used, which predefined difficulties
occurred and which were later corrected (D3). Second, adult summaries
without judgement (G.6): the products that analyse a child's work for
adults screen skills (Kaligo), compare with reference ranges (Dexteria) or
score handwriting against a threshold (Dynamilis), all of which BR-006
rules out, and none is described as separating attempts made with a guide
from attempts made without one (D4). Third, refinement that keeps the
drawing the child's own (G.4): the drawing-assistance features replace the
sketch with an artist's drawing (AutoDraw) or a perfect shape
(QuickShape), or refine handwritten text rather than drawings (Smart
Script), and none of the children's products describes refinement at all
(D6). Graded guidance (G.1, G.2) does exist, most closely in LetterSchool,
but in installed apps centred on letters and numbers; the one browser-based
tracing tool records nothing (D3, D5). Squigglr's differentiable value is
therefore the combination: graded drawing practice in a browser, records
that show the process behind each drawing, and adult summaries that report
that process without judging the child. Each part exists somewhere; no
product found offers them together. Whether supporting adults value this
combination is not yet confirmed: the needs come from SRC-001 and SRC-005,
and the value of refinement rests on ASM-011, which the specialists'
questionnaire answers (SRC-009.01) support but do not establish.

\paragraph{Products that must not be used.}
No stakeholder has ruled out a product. Two observations follow from the
business rules; they are not decisions. A product whose adult reports
screen, score or compare children (Kaligo's screening, Dexteria's
reference ranges, Dynamilis's profile) conflicts with BR-006, so it could
not be offered to adults as Squigglr's records. A product that replaces
the child's drawing (AutoDraw) conflicts with BR-005. Services excluded by
their own terms are noted in the Reusable Components subsection.

\subsection{Reusable Components}
\label{sec:ots-components}
This subsection lists candidate building blocks: first approaches to
refining a creative drawing, then supporting libraries and services.
They are candidates, not mandates. The team chooses among them after the
proof-of-concept experiments, and this specification requires none of
them. The Development Plan names four criteria for choosing a refinement
approach: drawing preservation, output validity, response time and cost
(SRC-002). Three more come from this document. Refinement must stay
minimal, with every refined point within \texttt{REFINE\_TOLERANCE} of the
original strokes (G.4, BR-005). The provider may change, so it should sit
behind one interface that takes stroke coordinates and returns adjusted
coordinates at the same scale (LC-004). The child's drawings should reach
as few outside parties as possible, in line with the supervisor's advice
to collect no information about children (SRC-004) while the privacy
requirements are still open (OI-02). Response times and request costs are
not known yet; the proof-of-concept experiment on model-based enhancement
will record them (SRC-002). Costs that are known appear in the Costs
section. Table~\ref{tab:ots-components} summarises the candidates.

\paragraph{Does refinement need AI?}
The supervisor asked whether the corrections need AI at all (SRC-004,
\href{https://github.com/AHMEDELZARIA/squigglr/issues/21}{\#21}). The
sources suggest that refinement as G.4 measures it does not. Geometric
smoothing and simplification adjust the child's own path within a stated
maximum error, need no training, and run in the browser, so the drawing
need not leave the device \citep{PaperJS2026Path,Agafonkin2013SimplifyJS}.
These methods do not know what a drawing depicts, so an adjustment that
depends on the item drawn needs recognition, and published methods
recognise strokes or infer geometric relations without a trained model
\citep{WobbrockEtAl2007,IgarashiEtAl1997}. A pretrained model adds the
ability to interpret a whole drawing, at the price of sending the drawing
to a service or hosting a model, slower responses, fees, and validating
every instruction before it is applied (SRC-002). The Development Plan
already tries geometric refinement first, describes it as geometric
processing rather than AI, and keeps it as the fallback if model work is
deferred (SRC-002).

\paragraph{Geometric refinement.}
The Paper.js library, which the Development Plan expects to use, smooths
paths by several methods, one of which (the centripetal Catmull--Rom
spline) guarantees no self-intersections. Its \texttt{simplify} method
fits as few curves as possible through a path's points within an allowed
maximum error, and is documented for point data from a mouse or touch
device \citep{PaperJS2026Path}. Paper.js is released under the
Massachusetts Institute of Technology (MIT) License
\citep{PaperJS2020License}. Simplify.js reduces the points of a line with
the Douglas--Peucker and radial-distance algorithms under a tolerance, and
is released under a Berkeley Software Distribution (BSD) licence
\citep{Agafonkin2013SimplifyJS}. A tolerance bounds the fitting error, but
whether every refined point stays within \texttt{REFINE\_TOLERANCE} of the
original strokes, as G.4 measures, still has to be checked on prepared
drawings.

\paragraph{Published stroke methods.}
Interactive beautification \citep{IgarashiEtAl1997} infers geometric
relations such as perpendicularity, congruence and symmetry between a new
stroke and existing ones, tidies one stroke at a time, and offers several
candidate results when the intent is ambiguous. Candidates could let the
child or the adult choose, but snapping to exact geometry may move points
further than \texttt{REFINE\_TOLERANCE} allows. The \$1 recogniser
\citep{WobbrockEtAl2007} matches a single stroke against stored templates
in about 100 lines of code, and in its authors' study exceeded 97\%
accuracy with one loaded template. A matcher of this kind could tell
whether a drawing is one of the agreed supported items (G.4) without
training or an outside service; drawings made of several strokes would
need more than this method offers.

\paragraph{Pretrained models.}
The Development Plan plans an existing pretrained model that accepts a
drawing preview and returns structured drawing instructions, which
Squigglr validates before applying; no model has been selected and no
custom training is planned (SRC-002). The candidates found fall into three
kinds.
\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{Hosted multimodal models}, reached through an application
  programming interface (API). The OpenAI API accepts images as input
  \citep{OpenAI2026DataControls}, and its Structured Outputs feature makes
  responses follow a JavaScript Object Notation (JSON) schema supplied by
  the developer \citep{OpenAI2026StructuredOutputs}, which suits
  instructions that must be validated. The Anthropic API also accepts
  images \citep{Anthropic2026Vision}. Their privacy terms differ. OpenAI
  does not train on API data by default and keeps abuse-monitoring logs
  for up to 30 days unless the customer is approved for zero data
  retention \citep{OpenAI2026DataControls}; its guidance says developers
  should not process personal data of children under 13 without first
  setting up zero data retention \citep{OpenAI2026Under18}. Anthropic may
  not train models on customer content \citep{Anthropic2025CommercialTerms},
  deletes API inputs and outputs within 30 days except in listed cases
  such as enforcing its usage policy \citep{Anthropic2026Retention}, and
  asks organisations whose products minors use to add safety measures,
  follow child-privacy law and disclose that users are interacting with
  AI \citep{Anthropic2026Minors}. Both charge by usage; fees are not
  estimated here.
  \item \emph{Open-weight vision-language models} that the team could host
  itself, such as Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct. Its model card lists the Apache
  License 2.0 and describes locating objects with bounding boxes or points
  and giving stable JSON output for coordinates \citep{Qwen2026VL7B}.
  Self-hosting keeps drawings away from outside providers but needs a
  machine with a graphics processing unit (GPU), which the team would run
  and pay for; the model card's examples load the model onto one.
  \item \emph{Stroke-based generative models}, such as Sketch-RNN, which
  generates vector drawings and was released with trained models and
  code in Google's Magenta project \citep{Eck2017SketchRNN} under the
  Apache License 2.0 \citep{Magenta2016License}. The model's training
  data is the Quick, Draw! dataset of 50 million drawings in 345
  categories, stored as timestamped strokes and licensed under Creative
  Commons Attribution 4.0 (CC BY 4.0) \citep{Google2017QuickDraw}.
  Output in strokes stays editable, but the model generates drawings
  rather than adjusting the child's, so its fit with BR-005 is doubtful.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Supporting libraries and services.}
The Development Plan expects Paper.js with Pointer Events for drawing
input, Supabase for records and adult authentication, and Vercel for
hosting (SRC-002). Pointer Events is a World Wide Web Consortium (W3C)
Recommendation for hardware-independent input from a mouse, pen or
touchscreen \citep{W3C2026PointerEvents}; which devices and browsers
Squigglr supports is still open (OI-09). Supabase provides a Postgres
database, authentication and file storage, is open source under the Apache
License 2.0, and can be self-hosted \citep{Supabase2026GitHub}; the
self-hosting option bears on data isolation between institutions (OI-06,
\href{https://github.com/AHMEDELZARIA/squigglr/issues/75}{\#75}). Vercel's
Hobby plan is free but limited to non-commercial personal use, and when a
usage limit is exceeded the feature usually stays unavailable until 30
days have passed \citep{Vercel2026Hobby}.
